| **D-092** | **Golden baseline for the accepted Ridderdragt — the armour's appearance is now enforced by CI (2026-08-02).** Test-only; unblocked by the D-091 acceptance and deliberately withheld until then, because a baseline committed earlier would have made the suite bless an appearance nobody had reviewed. **(1) WHAT THE MATRIX IS AND WHY ITS COLUMNS ARE THESE:** `tests/avatar-r2-torso-golden.spec.ts`, one `golden:` test rendering a 3×4 grid — **`no item` · `Ridderdragt` · `Ridderdragt + headwear + eyes`** across the four D-071 sizes **180×270 · 112×168 · 72×108 · 52×78**. The first two columns exist because of the observation D-091 recorded: at the smallest sizes the armour's silhouette approaches the base tee's, and **`legible-at-render-sizes` structurally cannot see that** — it measures COVERAGE of the mandatory region, not DISTINGUISHABILITY from wearing nothing. Putting "nothing equipped" beside "armour" at every size makes exactly that difference the thing under review, so a future regression in it fails loudly instead of passing a coverage check. The third column guards the z-order (garment z1 under every face layer, far under hair 40 / headwear 45). **(2) THE SCREENSHOT IS THE LAST LINE, NOT THE ONLY ONE:** before any pixel comparison the spec asserts per cell — `renderPath = "r2"`, the R2 WebP present (and **absent** in the `no item` column), **no C2 armour SVG leak**, garment `z === 1` read from the layer's own inline style (the computed value would reflect the surrounding stacking context, not the layer's z — the first draft got this wrong and was corrected), and zero broken images. **(3) CONVENTIONS FOLLOWED, NOT INVENTED:** self-served + fixture-intercepted (no real backend), chromium only, test named `golden:` so the regen workflow's `--grep` finds it, per-platform baselines (`-chromium-win32` generated locally, `-chromium-linux` on the CI platform), `animations: "disabled"` + `reducedMotion: "reduce"` per the determinism lesson, and a wait for every image to finish decoding before the baseline is taken. **(4) THE LINUX BASELINE CAME FROM THE SANCTIONED WORKFLOW, NOT FROM A HAND-EDIT:** `.github/workflows/update-avatar-goldens.yml` gained the spec in its regen list (one added line; the workflow stays manual-only and still commits nothing), was dispatched on this branch, and its artifact supplied `r2-torso-matrix-chromium-linux.png`. A Windows-rendered PNG would not match what CI compares against, so hand-copying the local baseline would have produced a permanently red gate. **(5) A DRIFT CHECK CAME FREE AND IS WORTH RECORDING:** the artifact regenerates **all** avatar goldens, so every one was diffed against what is committed — **46 identical, 0 differing, 1 new**. No existing baseline had drifted, and only the torso file was taken from the artifact; the other 46 were deliberately left untouched. **(6) BOUNDARIES:** test-only — no runtime, asset, manifest, catalog, DB, migration, RLS or Edge-Function change; **`AVATAR_R2` stays `false`**; pilot status stays `PILOT_WAVE_1_IN_PROGRESS`; the accepted artwork and the D-089 asset are untouched. The only non-test file changed is the manual regen workflow's spec list. D-071…D-091 not rewritten. Preserves D-032/D-033/D-042…D-091. (branch `feat/avatar-r2-torso-golden-d092`) (2026-08-02) |

| **D-093** | **Pilot Wave 2 DEFINED (not started) + the Wave-1 selection criteria corrected. Docs-only; no activation (2026-08-02).** Status **`PILOT_WAVE_2_DEFINED — NOT STARTED`**; overall pilot status stays `PILOT_WAVE_1_IN_PROGRESS`. **(1) THE REQUEST THAT PROMPTED THIS WAS REFUSED AS PHRASED, and the refusal is the substance:** "activate `AVATAR_R2` for wave 2" is not achievable, because **`AVATAR_R2` is a GLOBAL switch, not a wave selector** — `isAvatarR2()` returns true for *every* browser when the flag is true, and there is no cohort, percentage or DB targeting anywhere in the code. `docs/167a-phase1-pilot-rollout.md` §6 already forbids it in writing ("**Do not flip `AVATAR_R2 = true`** … not a pilot"), and D-075 rejected the client-side allowlist alternative because it would ship children's identifiers to the public bundle. Waves are enabled the way Wave 1 was: **per-browser opt-in**, zero PII, no code change. **(2) §2 WAS STALE AND IS CORRECTED — a real documentation defect, not a wording tweak:** the selection criteria still claimed the slot-gate renders "**only aura/back**", which stopped being true at **D-079** (headwear), **D-080** (eyes), **D-081** (face) and **D-090** (torso). Wave 1's cohort was therefore screened against a superseded rule. Harmless in practice — excluding cosmetics is *stricter* than reality — but it would have wrongly excluded eligible users from Wave 2. §2 now states what the runtime actually does, including that `torso` is gated **per item** (`armor-knight` only) and that `neck`/`body`/any unwired torso item still force whole-avatar C2. The decisive point recorded there: **which cosmetics a user owns now changes only WHETHER they see R2, never whether they see their items** — that is the D-083 guarantee, and it is what makes it safe to stop screening on cosmetics at all. **(3) WHY WAVE 2 EXISTS:** Wave 1's cohort was deliberately selected to own **no cosmetics**, so it exercises the bare figure only. Everything wired since — headwear, glasses, masks, and above all the Ridderdragt (D-085…D-092) — is **unobserved by a real user in a real browser**. Wave 2 is the cosmetic wave; it adds a new *dimension*, not a new *scale*. **(4) BINDING ENTRY CONDITION (§7b.1):** Wave 2 may not begin until Wave 1 reaches a §13 classification of `PILOT_PASS` or `PILOT_PASS_WITH_DEBT`, its §9 exposure is met (7 days, ≥3 sessions, ≥1 observed session each on quiz/hub/avatar), **no `BLOCKING` and no unresolved `MAJOR` finding** remains, and the owner records an explicit **Wave 2 GO** in §15. **Wave 1 is at 1 participant of target 3 — it is not complete**, so Wave 2 cannot start today. A `PILOT_FAIL` or an active §12 abort closes the track rather than advancing it. **(5) SCOPE (§7b.2):** target 3, max 5 — same shape as Wave 1; one browser/device per user; onboarded one at a time through the §8 persistent-browser gate; **at least one participant must own and equip the Ridderdragt**, or Wave 2 fails to test the thing it exists for; at least one should own a headwear/eyes/face item so the re-seated slots are observed beside the swapped-asset slot. **(6) WHAT IT MUST OBSERVE (§7b.3):** the garment on the R2 figure across all three surfaces (not the C2 SVG), no mixed stack, repeated equip/unequip leaving no stale layer, correct behaviour at the user's real sizes — with the **D-091 small-size observation explicitly marked as a known, accepted characteristic and NOT a defect**, so a pilot user noticing it does not read as a regression — other cosmetics composing correctly, and, if it occurs, the D-083 whole-avatar C2 fallback for a `neck`/`body`/unwired-torso item, which is correct behaviour worth seeing once. **(7) WAVE-2-SPECIFIC ABORT TRIGGERS (§7b.4):** an R2 figure rendering **without** an equipped item (the D-082 defect returning) · the armour on wrong anatomy or clipping the arms · a partial stack with any cosmetic equipped · the garment failing to load without whole-avatar fallback · equip/unequip leaving a stale layer. **(8) MECHANISM UNCHANGED, DELIBERATELY:** per-browser opt-in only; **`AVATAR_R2` stays `false`**; no allowlist (`NO_ALLOWLIST_FOR_PILOT`, D-075 — a server-side eligibility flag is the only sanctioned path to central enablement, and it is its own audited track); no UI toggle, no DB cohort, no percentage rollout. **(9) OWNER DECISIONS LEFT OPEN and recorded as such** in a Wave-2 GO log in §15: Wave 1's final classification, the GO itself, participant count (proposed target 3 / max 5), duration (proposed 7 days per §9), and who supplies a Ridderdragt-owning participant. **(10) BOUNDARIES:** docs-only — no code, runtime, asset, manifest, test, DB, migration, RLS, Edge-Function or workflow change; **no participant onboarded**; **no browser opted in**; participant log unchanged apart from the empty GO template; `AVATAR_R2` `false`; overall pilot status unchanged. D-071…D-092 not rewritten. Preserves D-032/D-033/D-042…D-092. (branch `docs/d093-pilot-wave-2`) (2026-08-02) |

| **D-094** | **The unit suite now runs in CI — closing a gap that made every avatar guarantee a local-only promise (2026-08-02).** **(1) THE GAP:** from D-085 until now, `npm run test:unit` **never ran in CI**; `playwright.yml` ran only the change classifier and Playwright. So the D-083 whole-avatar C2 fallback, D-090's per-item gating and mandatory garment layer, and the D-089 asset SHA pin were enforced **only on one developer's machine**. A regression in any of them could merge green. **(2) THE D-085 ASSUMPTION WAS WRONG, AND MEASURING IT WAS THE WHOLE JOB:** D-085 §5.2 deferred the fix because "some tests need a vendored libwebp binary and the runners are Linux". Measured instead of assumed — hide `tools/avatar/vendor/`, run the suite — **316 of 328 tests passed without it; exactly 12 failed, in exactly 2 files**. **96 % of the suite could have run in CI all along.** **(3) MECHANISM:** a new `unit` job in `playwright.yml` (ubuntu, `npm ci`, `npm run test:unit:ci`, 10-min timeout) plus `tests/run-unit-ci.mjs`, a tiny runner that enumerates `tests/unit/*.test.mjs` and drops the excluded files. A runner rather than a shell glob because the exclusion must read identically on Windows and Linux **and live in one place a test can assert against** (`tests/unit-ci-exclusions.mjs`). **(4) THE JOB DELIBERATELY TAKES NO SUPABASE LOCK:** it is pure Node — no network, no backend, no browser — so it must not join `e2e-shared-supabase`. That group serializes runs against one live test student, and a queued run in it **can be displaced and cancelled outright**, as happened to `7668a16` on 2026-08-02 when three merges landed within 19 minutes. A unit job that queued behind live-backend work would inherit that fragility for nothing. **(5) ANTI-DRIFT GUARD — the part that makes this last:** `tests/unit/ci-unit-coverage.test.mjs` (7 tests) asserts every excluded file exists, that each **genuinely** drives a vendored binary (so the list cannot become a parking space for merely inconvenient tests), that **no file outside the list** depends on one, that the list is still exactly 2 entries, that the workflow really invokes the runner and has a `unit` job, and that the job takes no Supabase lock. **Verified by construction, not by assertion:** a probe file naming `dwebp.exe` was dropped into `tests/unit/`, and the guard failed with the file named in the message before the probe was removed. **(6) WHAT IS STILL NOT COVERED — measured and recorded, not glossed:** exclusion is **per FILE**, so **54 tests are excluded to avoid 12**; the other **42 are CI-capable but do not run**. Among them are guarantees named in §1 as the motivation — the D-089 asset SHA pin and the D-085 mask-template integrity tests live inside the two excluded files. **CI now runs 281 of 335 tests (84 %), not 96 %.** The remedy is known and small: split the 12 binary-dependent tests into their own `*.binary.test.mjs` files, which would take CI to ~323 of 335. That is deliberately a **separate** change — it edits two audited test files, and doing it as a side effect of wiring up CI would mix a coverage decision into an infrastructure one. **(7) THE TWO EXCLUDED FILES ARE NOT WEAKENED:** they still run, and still **fail loudly**, locally; nothing was skipped, and the D-085 rule that a silently skipped asset gate is indistinguishable from a passing one is preserved. **(8) BOUNDARIES:** test/CI infrastructure only — no runtime, asset, manifest, catalog, DB, migration, RLS or Edge-Function change; `js/` untouched; **`AVATAR_R2` stays `false`**; pilot status unchanged. Local: full suite **335/335**, CI subset **281/281**. D-071…D-093 not rewritten. (branch `feat/unit-tests-in-ci-d094`) (2026-08-02) |
| **D-095** | **Garment-mesh POC built — and it found that the Ridderdragt's backfill is a MISSING SLEEVE, not a fitting error. Per-band coverage added to the harness (2026-08-02).** Status **`GARMENT_MESH_POC — POC_NEEDS_REVISION`** (the method is sound; it is the wrong tool for this particular gap). Tooling only: no runtime change, the accepted asset is untouched, `AVATAR_R2` stays `false`. **(1) WHAT WAS BUILT:** `tools/avatar/garment-mesh/mesh-core.mjs` — deterministic piecewise-affine warping with **inverse mapping** and barycentric coordinates (a forward map would leave holes wherever the mesh stretches), schema validation that refuses zero-area triangles, foldovers, unknown vertex ids and out-of-canvas vertices, declarative constraints (`pinnedVertices`, per-axis locks, horizontal/vertical groups, symmetry pairs) that are **enforced**, not merely declared, and per-triangle distortion metrics. Sampling is bilinear on **premultiplied** alpha — the same reason `extract-master-base.mjs` premultiplies: unpremultiplied interpolation drags transparent RGB into the garment edge and recreates the D-058/D-061 halo. Triangles are tested in declared order and the first containing triangle wins, so a pixel on a shared edge is never resolved by chance. **(2) THE SOURCE WAS CORRECTED MID-TASK, by the owner, and it mattered:** the first plan used the fitted+clipped `nobackfill` candidate. Material outside the edit boundary is permanently gone from that file, so a mesh could never recover it — an unfairly weak test of the method. The POC was rebuilt on the **raw generation** (`83fcff0c…`) with a deterministic cover placement (scale 0.4167, **no overscan** — overscan being the blunt instrument the mesh was meant to replace). **(3) FIRST RESULT — better numbers, worse picture, for the fifth time in this track:** mesh coverage 92.57 % vs the old fit's 88.32 % on the same placement; missing mandatory pixels 11,407 → 7,260, i.e. below the 8,608 the shipped asset actually backfilled. Zero foldovers, zero ink outside `edit`, zero on `protect`. **But the shoulder caps became pointed, cape-like flares** and a third component appeared. **(4) A SECOND TOPOLOGY WAS TRIED AND FAILED — with a proof, not an impression:** doubling to 81 vertices / 128 triangles produced **byte-identical distortion metrics** (median area-ratio 1.054622, p95 and max 1.789216 in both runs). The auto-fit scales every column by the same per-row factor, and subdividing a uniformly stretched span yields uniformly stretched sub-spans. The flare was never a concentration in one triangle; it is the row's own 1.70× stretch (mask 366 px vs artwork 215 px at y=680). **More vertices on the same rule cannot help — that was my error in offering it as an option.** **(5) THE ACTUAL FINDING, measured row by row:** at y=640 the artwork covers the mandatory region **completely** (350 px vs 346). By y=680 it is 205 px against a 366 px mask. **The armour's short sleeves end at y≈675–680; the base tee's reach y≈714.** The deficit is not misplaced garment, it is **absent garment** — and stretching a rounded sleeve hem down over a longer sleeve area is exactly what produced the flare. **This explains the shipped asset's 5,990 shoulder-band backfill pixels precisely.** D-086's art brief did ask for shoulder caps across 560–714; the generation did not deliver them; the harness measured *total* mandatory coverage, so backfill closed the hole before anyone asked why it was there. The amount was disclosed honestly in D-087 and accepted deliberately in D-088 — what was never traced is the **cause**. **(6) PER-BAND COVERAGE ADDED TO THE HARNESS (the durable fix):** `check-r2-torso-candidate.mjs` now measures hard-mask coverage per band (`collar`/`shoulder`/`torso`/`skirt` — the same bands the adapter attributes backfill to) **before any backfill**, always discloses it, and blocks below **60 %** via `no-band-largely-uncovered`. The floor sits deliberately BELOW the accepted asset's 87.95 % shoulder figure: the gate is there to catch a **missing garment part**, not to retroactively fail an owner-accepted asset whose shortfall was measured and disclosed. `REFERENCE_BAND_COVERAGE` records what actually shipped (collar 100 %, shoulder 87.95 %, torso 94.11 %, skirt 100 %) so every future candidate is comparable to it. **(7) IT CATCHES BOTH FAILURE SHAPES, verified:** the accepted asset still passes; the mesh output is **rejected** on `skirt 46.1 %` — because the mesh's aggregate "improvement" was bought by robbing other bands (collar 100 → 88.7 %, torso 94.1 → 89.6 %, skirt 100 → 46.1 % while shoulder rose 88.0 → 95.6 %). A single coverage figure hid that entirely. **(8) RECOMMENDATION:** the real remedy is the **source**, not the fit — a generation whose sleeves reach y≈714 as the brief already requires. A mesh would then be finishing work of a few percent, which is what it is genuinely good for. Mesh cannot invent material the drawing does not contain. **(9) TESTS:** `tests/unit/avatar-r2-torso-band-coverage.test.mjs` (8) — bands match the adapter's, every band always disclosed, a starved shoulder band blocks, a starved skirt blocks (the mesh's actual failure), the floor is asserted to sit below the accepted reference, the reference records what shipped, a full candidate passes, and band coverage uses the same OPAQUE contract as the mandatory-region gate. Local **unit 336/336, 0 skipped**. **(10) BOUNDARIES:** tooling + harness + docs only — no runtime, `js/`, manifest, catalog, DB, migration, RLS, Edge-Function, workflow or golden change; **the accepted `armor-knight-r2-v1.webp` is untouched**; no image generation, no API call, no AI in the deformation; `AVATAR_R2` stays `false`; pilot status unchanged. The mesh tooling is committed as the instrument that produced the finding, so the measurement is reproducible — not as a production path. D-071…D-094 not rewritten. (branch `tools/avatar-r2-garment-mesh-d095`) (2026-08-02) |

| **D-096** | **The D-095 band gate was defeated by the very mechanism it was built to expose — fixed, plus prompt versioning and a hard stop on overwriting accepted provenance (2026-08-03).** Tooling only; no runtime, asset, manifest, golden or pilot change; `AVATAR_R2` stays `false`. **(1) THE DEFECT, found within hours of D-095 merging:** `no-band-largely-uncovered` measured the candidate **as submitted** — and a candidate normally arrives with backfill already applied. Every band therefore read as covered and the gate passed. A prompt-v2 candidate whose collar band the model drew only **41.5 %** of scored `PASS_AUTOMATED`, because backfill had filled the other 58.5 %. The gate built to stop backfill hiding a missing garment part was hidden by backfill. The code comment even claimed "coverage BEFORE any backfill", which was simply untrue. **(2) THE FIX:** the adapter's sidecar already records backfill per band, so the drawn coverage is `covered − backfilled`, clamped at zero. Both figures are now reported — `asSubmitted` is what the file contains, `drawn` is what the image model actually produced — and **the blocking decision uses `drawn`**. Verified in both directions: the v2 candidate now **REJECTs** on `collar 41.5 % drawn`, and the **owner-accepted asset still PASSes** (87.95 % drawn, above the 60 % floor) — the gate catches missing garment without retroactively failing approved work. **(3) MISSING PROVENANCE IS NOW STATED, NOT ASSUMED:** without a sidecar the split is unknowable, so the report carries `provenanceKnown: false` and says the figure **OVERSTATES** what was drawn. Treating "no evidence of filling" as "nothing was filled" is the original mistake in another form. **(4) PROMPT VERSIONING (`PROMPT_V1`/`V2`/`V3`, `PROMPT_VERSION`):** v1 is kept verbatim because it produced the accepted candidate; a candidate can now be traced to the exact wording that made it. **(5) A NEAR-MISS WORTH RECORDING — versioned output paths + a hard stop:** the generator wrote to fixed filenames, so running it again would have overwritten **in place** the two files that are the accepted asset's provenance: `torso-armor-knight-raw.png` (`83fcff0c…`, non-reproducible — the model call is not deterministic) and `torso-armor-knight-candidate.png` (`31f4b2b6…`, owner-accepted in D-088). Output names now carry the prompt version, and `assertNotProtected()` refuses those two paths outright on every write. The owner's out-of-repo backup would have made it recoverable, but recoverable is not a licence to overwrite approved work. **(6) WHAT THE THREE GENERATIONS ESTABLISHED, and why no v4 is planned:** v2 fixed the sleeves the D-095 diagnosis pointed at (shoulder backfill 5,990 → 3,809) but broke the collar (100 → 41.5 %) and skirt (100 → 56.5 %); v3 repaired the skirt (100 %) and the torso (94.7 %, better than v1) and improved the shoulder (90.0 %), but the collar stayed low (45.6 %). Totals: v1 **8,608** · v2 10,486 · v3 9,172. **No regeneration beat the accepted asset overall** — each fix moved the shortfall to another band rather than removing it. The likely cause is instruction ranking: v2's "SLEEVE LENGTH IS THE MOST IMPORTANT REQUIREMENT" taught the model the other constraints were negotiable, and v3's unranked "three equally mandatory" set recovered the skirt but not the collar. Prompt wording has stopped being the productive lever. **(7) THE v2/v3 CANDIDATES ARE NOT TRACKED** and not promoted — they remain gitignored build artefacts, kept only as measurement inputs. **(8) TESTS:** five new regression tests in `avatar-r2-torso-band-coverage.test.mjs` (13 total) — a band the model barely drew is blocked even when backfill filled it, the disclosure separates drawn from submitted, missing provenance is reported as unknown, the accepted asset's real split (5,990/2,618) still passes, and a sidecar cannot claim more backfill than there are pixels. Local **unit 348/348, 0 skipped**; CI subset **294**. D-071…D-095 not rewritten. (branch `fix/torso-harness-drawn-coverage-d096`) (2026-08-03) |

| **D-097** | **The shoulder-band shortfall traced to its end: a sleeve-donor challenger BEATS the accepted asset, and the owner's fabric-underlayer reframing reduces "repair" backfill by 88 % — but the last 3 columns need ~10 px of new plate artwork. Design/measurement only (2026-08-03).** No image generation, no runtime change, nothing promoted, the accepted asset untouched, `AVATAR_R2` stays `false`. **(1) PART ONE — SLEEVE-DONOR CHALLENGER, `CHALLENGER_READY_FOR_OWNER_REVIEW`:** since three regenerations could not beat v1 overall (D-096), this grafts ONLY the left and right sleeve regions of the prompt-v3 generation onto the accepted v1 artwork. Result vs the accepted asset: collar **100 %**, shoulder **88.0 → 98.5 %**, torso 94.1 %, skirt 100 %, **missing 8,608 → 3,364 (−61 %)**, sleeve reach y 676 → 709. **Everything outside the two sleeve masks is BYTE-IDENTICAL to v1 — 0 differing pixels, proven and rendered as a diff image containing no red.** No donor pixels on breastplate, belt or skirt. **(2) THE SEAM TOOK THREE ATTEMPTS, AND THE FIRST TWO WERE MY WRONG ASSUMPTION:** both assumed the armhole sat at the torso-column edge (x 401). Cut there, the join was a dead-straight paste edge (median luminance step 0.81 → 50.67 against an untouched-v1 control). A "plate-following" tracker then locked onto a dark pixel and held position, producing another straight line 22 px further out and a **worse** step (75.69). Only when the luma PROFILE was actually read — sleeve ~149, dark stroke, breastplate ~113 — did the armhole appear at **x 410–440, INBOARD of the torso column**: the column was the skirt's width, not the breastplate's. Cutting on that measured curve gave median 28.97 against a 23.17 control, i.e. **a join no more visible than the garment's own armhole line**. **(3) AND ONE MORE SELF-INFLICTED FAILURE, worth recording:** a straight replace lost 170 opaque pixels the base had, so torso finished 2 px worse than v1 and failed its criterion. The obvious fix — keep the higher alpha — was tried and **broke the picture**: 3,131 pixels have both artworks opaque but differing by one alpha step (255 vs 254), so the base won arbitrarily and the two drawings interleaved at **22 source switches per 100 px**, visible as salt-and-pepper speckle at 8×. The durable rule is asymmetric: **the donor owns the pixel wherever it is OPAQUE** (so the sleeve face comes from ONE drawing) and the base survives only where the donor is not — 0 speckle, 1.1 switches per 100 px, 0 lost pixels. **(4) PART TWO — THE OWNER'S REFRAMING (2026-08-03):** "the shoulder guard should of course be on top of the backfill, so the backfill is given meaning". That inverts the pipeline: not artwork → clip → patch, but **fabric first, plates over it**. It is not a rename of the old fill — the shipped backfill copies the NEAREST NEIGHBOUR colour, has no intent, and is applied LAST where nothing can cover it; here the fabric is an explicit region in ONE deliberate tone (derived from v1's own sleeve palette, median RGB 83/87/90) with the accepted artwork composited OVER it, so every plate edge and outline belongs to the approved drawing. **(5) THE SPLIT THAT MAKES IT HONEST:** of v1's 8,608 mandatory-gap pixels, **7,559 lie OUTBOARD of the measured armhole seam** — sleeve, i.e. legitimately fabric — and **1,049 lie inboard**, torso/plate territory, which stays classified as **repair**, not laundered into "fabric". So `residualRepairBackfillPx` falls **8,608 → 1,049 (−88 %)** without relabelling anything. Three fabric tones were built (neutral, cool steel, lightly shaded); at 8× the shipped backfill is visibly mottled where all three are uniform. **(6) PART THREE — PLATE MICRO-FIT, and a metric of mine that overcounted:** the owner rejected the first fabric POC on sight (square protrusions, ragged cuff, exposed fabric top) and pushed back on my claim that the plate needed 20+ px of redrawing — correctly, since that figure came from superseded geometry. Redesigning the fabric as a **deliberate sleeve silhouette with a rounded cuff** (possible because v1 is composited last, so fabric under v1 is invisible) removed the blocks; a local mesh then moved the plate's outer column **8 px out and 8 px down**, with the inner edge, collar side and everything ≥20 px inboard pinned. Max edge-length ratio **1.083** and **0 foldovers** — the plate is moved, not stretched. Exposed columns fell **28 → 14 → 3**. **My exposure metric was also counting the garment's OWN SILHOUETTE edge as exposed**; with silhouette and true cut separated, 8 of 12 flagged columns were the mask's own boundary. **(7) WHAT REMAINS — 3 columns, not 28:** at x 338, 339, 687 the fabric starts 10 rows below the mask top with neither plate nor fabric between. Closing them needs the shoulder plate's outermost lower edge drawn **~10 px deeper** — three columns, ten rows. That is a small illustrator correction, not a redraw, and it is the honest end of what composition alone can do. Verdict `FABRIC_UNDERLAYER_NEEDS_NEW_PLATE_ARTWORK`. **(8) BOUNDARIES:** design and measurement only; no image generation, no API call, no runtime/`js/`/manifest/asset/golden/catalog/DB change; the accepted `armor-knight-r2-v1.webp` and its provenance untouched; pilot status unchanged. Five review packages under `_avatar-artefakter\D097-*`; the tooling is committed as the instrument that produced the findings, so every number here is reproducible. Local **unit 348/348, 0 skipped**. D-071…D-096 not rewritten. (branch `docs/d097-sleeve-donor-fabric-underlayer`) (2026-08-03) |

| **D-098** | **D-097 §7 was wrong: no new shoulder-plate artwork is needed. The three "cut columns" were false positives of my own exposure metric, and the fix is the classifier — measured, not argued (2026-08-03).** Status **`D098_EXPOSURE_CLASSIFIER_CORRECTED — NO_PLATE_ARTWORK_REQUIRED`**. Tooling + tests + docs only; no artwork change, no image generation, no API call, no runtime/`js/`/manifest/asset/mask/fixture/golden/workflow/DB change; the accepted `armor-knight-r2-v1.webp` untouched; `AVATAR_R2` stays `false`; pilot status unchanged. **This entry supersedes exactly ONE D-097 claim — §7's "at x 338, 339, 687 the fabric starts 10 rows below the mask top… closing them needs the shoulder plate's outermost lower edge drawn ~10 px deeper… a small illustrator correction". Nothing else in D-097 is rewritten; its sleeve-donor result, the fabric-underlayer reframing and the 8,608 → 1,049 split all stand.** **(1) THE CORRECTION WAS MEASURED BEFORE IT WAS DRAWN, AND IT DID NOT SURVIVE:** the whole proposed fix is **10 pixels** — 4 at x=338 (y 652–655), 4 at x=339, 2 at x=687 (y 654–655). **All 10 lie outside `torso-edit-allowed-v1` and on `torso-protect-v1`.** The mask spec's own gate `protect-is-complement-of-edit` passes with 0 px, so those are the same statement — and "0 ink outside edit" and "0 ink on protect" are binding D-085/D-090 rules. Nobody may paint there, **an illustrator included: it is a mask-contract boundary, not a skill boundary.** **(2) AND 8 OF THE 10 ARE ALREADY DRAWN:** at x=338 the micro-fit plate is opaque at y 652/653/654 and 207/255 at y 655 — exactly the rows the correction was meant to add. The composite is empty there because the last composition step clips to the edit mask. **Adding artwork at those coordinates produces the identical result: erased.** **(3) WHY THEY LOOKED LIKE CUTS — MULTI-RUN MASK TOPOLOGY:** a column's mandatory mask can be **discontinuous**. At x=338 it runs y 646–649 and again y 656–693, with rows 650–655 outside the garment altogether; the plate covers the first run, the fabric fills the second, and the rows between belong to neither because the garment does not exist there. **100 of the 370 mandatory columns are multi-run** — the silhouette wobbles in and out row to row. **(4) MY METRIC'S BUG WAS IN TWO PLACES, WHICH IS WHY FIXING IT ONCE DID NOT HOLD.** D-097 §6 already caught half of it (silhouette vs cut, 12 → 3). Still wrong: **(a)** it compared a fabric top only against `maskTop`, the column's **FIRST** run, so the top of a **later** run — equally the garment's own outer edge — still read as a cut (x=338, x=339); **(b)** it never asked whether the fabric pixel was inside the mandatory mask **at all**, so fabric placed beyond it by the deliberately generous sleeve shape read as a cut (x=687). **(5) THREE CATEGORIES, AND ONLY ONE IS A FAULT:** **A `mandatory_run_silhouette`** — inside the mask, at most one row below **its own run's** top: the garment's legitimate outer edge, not a fault. **B `non_mandatory_fabric`** — the fabric top is outside the mandatory mask: **not** a cut, but **not** relabelled as silhouette either — it is a real open question about the fabric mask's shape and is reported separately by contract. **C `true_cut`** — inside the mask, more than one row below its own run's top. **THE THRESHOLD IS UNCHANGED AND STILL EXACTLY ZERO CATEGORY-C COLUMNS.** What changed is which pixels the classifier inspects. **Category C = 0 in the micro-fit, in the plate-fit, and in the do-nothing baseline**; the micro-fit's category B is x 687 and x 692. **(6) THE RULE NOW LIVES IN ONE PLACE, BECAUSE IT WAS DUPLICATED IN THREE:** `fabric-underlayer-poc.mjs` counted every uncovered top (28), `fabric-plate-fit.mjs` did the same walk with no silhouette test (10), and `plate-microfit.mjs` added the first-run test and reported the 3 that became §7. All three now import `tools/avatar/garment-mesh/fabric-exposure.mjs` — a pure module with no file IO, no pixel access and **no threshold parameter**, so there is no seam through which the gate can be widened. It refuses a non-boolean mask outright: handing it alpha would let antialiasing invent runs, which is the failure it exists to prevent. **(7) TESTS — 21 in `tests/unit/avatar-r2-fabric-exposure.test.mjs`,** on synthetic masks whose topology the test states outright AND on the real tracked mask fixtures at the real coordinates: first-run top is A, **later**-run top is A (with the legacy rule asserted to get it wrong, so the regression is pinned as data), deep-inside-run is C, fabric between runs is never C, fabric outside the mask is B, x=338 and x=339 are not C, x=687 is B, category C is 0 across every measured fabric top, **the legacy rule reproduces exactly `[338, 339, 687]`**, the threshold is 0 and not caller-configurable, category B is a required report field, protect is still the exact complement of edit, and the 10 proposed pixels are asserted unpaintable while the classifier is asserted to export no way to write a pixel. Runs in CI — pure JS, no vendored binary. Local **unit 369/369, 0 skipped; CI subset 315/315**. **(8) WHAT IS STILL OPEN AND IS NOT FIXED HERE:** **R0** category B (x 687, 692) — fabric beyond the mandatory region with an uncovered top edge; **R1** the micro-fit composite has **2 opaque components** (a 1-px stray warp pixel at (337, 654) survives the edit clip); **R2** residual repair **1,635 px** against the 1,049 target; **R3** the fabric underlayer is 6 components with 3 specks. All four are real, all four are out of scope for a classifier correction, and none is laundered by it. **(9) BOUNDARIES:** the review package is `_avatar-artefakter\D098-shoulder-plate-micro-artwork-review\` and stays out of the repo; `plate-edge-artwork.mjs` is tracked as the instrument that produced these numbers and defines **no** classification rule of its own. D-071…D-097 not rewritten. (branch `fix/avatar-r2-exposure-classifier-d098`) (2026-08-03) |

| **D-099** | **Pilot Wave 1 participant #2 ONBOARDED through the persistent-browser gate; Wave 1 moves to 2 of target 3. Creating a third would have been the parallel account-creation path the task forbade, plus a redundant row in production auth. **Eligibility nuance recorded rather than silently normalised:** `skin_tone` is **absent** from this profile and resolves to `medium` through the documented `skinToneFor()` default (`js/avatar-layers.js`), so the manifest key `neutral-medium` resolves and §2 is satisfied — participant #1 has the field set explicitly. No profile write was made to cosmetically align them. **(2) PROOF (all §6 criteria):** opt-in activated; **Fase A** — `renderPath=r2`, six layers (`base, blush, face, iris, eyes, hair-r2`), `hasMandatoryBase=true`, no C2 `.svg` mixing, no broken images on **avatar, hub and quiz**; **persistence gate** — browser **fully closed (normal, not forced)**, **same profile reopened**, **opt-in survived** and `renderPath` stayed `r2` with the same six-layer stack on all three surfaces; **opt-out demonstrated** (complete C2 render, base `body-neutral-medium-c2.svg`, app functional) **and the opt-in restored clean**. Blink and breathing confirmed by the owner's §7 visual check at 100 % at real render size; no visible raster fringe. **(3) A FALSE START WAS CAUGHT BY MEASUREMENT, NOT BY EYE — AND IT IS WHY IDENTITY IS NOW VERIFIED BEFORE A RENDER COUNTS AS EVIDENCE.** Two early attempts ran against the **wrong account** (a stale, server-revoked session, then participant #1). Both produced a perfectly clean `renderPath=r2` with six layers, because `baseSrcForR2()` falls back to `neutral`/`medium` for a null identity — **an R2 render on an empty profile is pixel-indistinguishable from a correct one.** The gate evidence for this row was therefore taken only after the session's account and `avatar_identity` were read back and matched. **(4) SEPARATE PRE-EXISTING OBSERVATION, NOT AN R2 DEFECT AND NOT FIXED HERE:** with an expired/revoked session `avatar.html` still renders a default avatar, because `loadAll()` issues the profile query with `.maybeSingle()` and **does not check `error`**, so a failed load silently becomes the default identity. The C2 path behaves identically — the fallback lives in the identity resolvers, not in R2. Severity `INFO`; its own track. **(5) STATUS CHANGE:** participant #2 → **`ONBOARDED`**; Wave 1 **1 → 2 of target 3** (max 5). Overall status stays `PILOT_WAVE_1_IN_PROGRESS` — **§9 exposure (7 calendar days, ≥3 real sessions each) is not met for either participant**, so no §13 classification is available on participant count alone. **(6) PRIVACY:** the log records only the `W1-P2` label, the `TEST_STUDENT2` account-convention name, browser (Chrome), device (desktop), origin and date — **no UID, email, token or password, and no browser-storage key/value**. **(7) BOUNDARIES:** docs-only (`167a-phase1-pilot-rollout.md` status line + §7 + §7b.1 + §15 row 2 + capture sheet + next-step; this register; `ROADMAP.md`); **no code, runtime, asset, manifest, test, backend, database, account-profile or user-record change**; `AVATAR_R2` stays **`false`**; **Wave 2 stays `DEFINED — NOT STARTED`**; broad activation remains unauthorised. (branch `docs/r2-pilot-wave1-participant2`) (2026-08-08) |

Wave 1's question ("does the bare R2 figure render correctly and persist across a real browser restart?") has now been answered twice with measured, not eyeballed, proof. **(2) THIS IS PERMITTED BY THE DOCUMENT AS WRITTEN, NOT A DEVIATION:** §7 sets a "**target** 3 … **maximum** 5 without a new owner decision" — only the *ceiling* was ever gated on a new owner decision, and §13's classifications specify **no participant count at all**. Reducing the target is an owner call; it is recorded here rather than applied silently. **(3) WHAT THIS DECISION DOES NOT DO — AND THE DISTINCTION MATTERS:** it does **not** close Wave 1, does **not** classify it under §13, and **does not waive §9**. | **D-101** | **OWNER DECISION — the R2 pilot is DISCONTINUED, not passed, and R2 becomes the default render (2026-08-08).** Status **`PILOT_DISCONTINUED_BY_OWNER`**; `AVATAR_R2` `false` → **`true`**. **(1) WHAT THE OWNER DECIDED:** the pilot is not carried further; the owner checked the current R2 build themselves and accepts its function and visual result; the remaining pilot period and the remaining §9 exposure are **waived by explicit owner decision, on grounds of time**. **(2) THIS IS NOT A PASS, AND THE RECORD SAYS SO:** **no §13 classification was reached or awarded** — not `PILOT_PASS`, not `PILOT_PASS_WITH_DEBT`. §9 was **WAIVED, not satisfied**: neither participant ever reached 7 calendar days, ≥3 real app sessions, or ≥1 observed session each on quiz/hub/avatar. **§9's text is unchanged** — it was overridden by decision, not edited into compliance. The closing acceptance is the **owner's manual check**, which is a legitimate but **weaker evidence base** than the one the pilot was designed to produce, and that difference is recorded rather than smoothed over. **No pilot session data was invented, reconstructed or back-dated, and no earlier FAIL / PAUSED / CONDITIONAL result has been rewritten to PASS.** **(3) WHAT THE PILOT DID ESTABLISH, PRESERVED UNCHANGED:** two participants genuinely passed the full §8 persistent-browser gate — #1 on 2026-07-27 (D-078), #2 on 2026-08-08 (D-099, six-layer stack on avatar/hub/quiz, no mixed stack, no broken layers, opt-in survived a full browser restart, opt-out demonstrated). That is **two point-in-time onboarding runs, not longitudinal use**. **(4) WHAT IT NEVER ESTABLISHED:** no sustained real use; **no cosmetic was ever observed by a pilot user** — Wave 1's cohort owned nothing and Wave 2 never started, so the Ridderdragt (D-085…D-092) and the re-seated headwear/eyes/face slots reach production with **zero real-user observation**; no mobile observation; only default-equivalent hairstyles exercised. **(5) THE ACTIVATION MECHANISM:** `isAvatarR2()` now honours **only** the exact string `"0"`, and only to force C2. The legacy pilot value `"1"` is **inert** — it falls through to the default like any other value, which is what makes a global rollback absolute (no browser can pin itself to R2 against the flag). Missing, empty, malformed or unreadable storage also falls through to the default, so the choice is deterministic and never throws. Eligibility only: `isAvatarR2ActiveFor()` still requires the full manifest stack, and the D-062 atomic gate and D-083 whole-avatar fallback are untouched. **(6) MEASURED BLAST RADIUS (read-only aggregate, 28 students, no identifiers):** **23 render R2** (`neutral`+`medium`), **5 stay C2** (`male`), 1 owns the R2-wired `armor-knight`, 7 have any cosmetic equipped. **KNOWN TRADE-OFF, NEVER PILOT-TESTED:** `hairSrcForR2()` ignores `identity.hairstyle` and always resolves `hair-northstar-v1.webp`, while C2 resolves one of **seven** selectable styles — so all 23 see the North Star hair regardless of choice. **1** of them stored an explicitly non-default style (`buzzcut`); the other 22 stored `default`/nothing. Hair **colour** is still tinted from the identity token; the **style** is fixed. This is a property of the build the owner accepted, not a regression introduced here — recorded so it is known rather than discovered. **(7) ROLLBACK:** per browser `localStorage.avatar_r2 = "0"` → complete C2; globally `AVATAR_R2 = false` + redeploy → C2 everywhere including stale keys. No database, migration or user-record change either way. **(8) BOUNDARIES:** `js/avatar-layers.js` (the switch + its contract), the directly affected unit tests (guards re-pointed from "flag stays false" to the activation/rollback contract; C2-path tests now select C2 by explicit opt-out), and docs (`167a-phase1-pilot-rollout.md` §16 + status, this register, `ROADMAP.md`, `157r-feature-flags.md`). **No asset, manifest, avatar design, Supabase, Edge Function, migration, pilot user or golden baseline changed; no telemetry added; Wave 2 remains `DEFINED — NOT STARTED` and is not started by this.** (branch `feat/avatar-r2-default-activation-d101`) (2026-08-08) |
| **D-102** | **AUDIT — the R2 render IGNORES the student's hairstyle; measured, options costed, decision REQUESTED (2026-08-17).** Status **`OPTION_A_PARTIALLY_LANDED — AFRO_ONLY — UI_NOTE_RETAINED`** (2026-08-29 — see D-114; was `OWNER_DECIDED_OPTION_D — UI_HONESTY_IMPLEMENTED, OPTION_A_DEFERRED`, owner 2026-08-20 — see (10); before that `DESIGN_READY_AWAITING_OWNER_DECISION`). Full document: [`167a-r2-hair-identity-audit.md`](./167a-r2-hair-identity-audit.md). **(1) THE DEFECT:** `hairSrcForR2(identity)` (`js/avatar-layers.js:443`) accepts `identity` and never reads it — every R2 render resolves `hair/hair-northstar-v1.webp`, while `hairSrcForC2` honours all seven styles plus the 155F legacy aliases. The avatar page still offers the choice, the RPC still validates and persists it, and hair **colour** still responds — so the panel reacts to one half of itself and silently discards the other. **This is an overridden choice, not a missing feature.** D-101 recorded it as a known trade-off when R2 became the default; it was never pilot-tested. **(2) THE MEASUREMENT (new read-only tool `npm run avatar:r2-hair-fit`, deterministic, NO AI, both runtime WebP inputs SHA-pinned, writes no file):** in the shared 160×240 canvas the R2 head is **dimensionally the C2 head** — skull width **60.0** vs 60, centre **80.5** vs 80, chin/neck **81.6** vs 80 — but its **crown sits 11.6 units LOWER** (31.6 vs 20): a shorter skull on the same jaw. Consequently, for all seven C2 hair assets — measured from their path data by exact line/quadratic-Bézier column crossings — **the misfit is primarily VERTICAL**: lateral extent already matches the R2 skull and no style needs a scale or horizontal shift. Three things are kept apart rather than merged. **(a)** The per-style mean column deltas fall between **+3.2 and +8.7 units** (`ponytail` +3.2, `curly` +3.9, `afro` +5.3, `tousled` +6.7, `short` +6.8, `buzz` +6.9, `long` +8.7). **(b)** A single shared `translateY(~2.8 %)` is therefore a **CANDIDATE** — because the means cluster, not because it has been tried. **(c)** It is **NOT VALIDATED**, per style or at all: every style's column spread is ~17–21 units, wider than the differences between the means it would replace, mostly the R2 reference hairline's own strand irregularity (it swings 33.8–50.3 across the forehead), which bounds the offset to roughly ±8 units. Whether one shared value, seven per-style values or neither is right needs a render-scale visual review with per-style residuals; **no such review has been run, and no shared tolerance is proposed**. This is the same mechanism as D-080's eyes (+4.4 %) and it explains why D-079's "hats align natively" says nothing about hair: a hat sits *near* the crown, hair must *meet the hairline*. **(3) THE HIDDEN BASE, ON AVERAGE COLOUR ONLY:** the decomposed base was cut UNDER the North Star hair, so the area that hair permanently hides was reconstructed rather than observed (hidden 20.0 % of the head, mean RGB 246.0/186.9/126.5 vs visible skin 245.5/184.5/123.7, **mean colour distance 3.7** on a 0–441 scale, comparable variance). **What that supports:** the hidden pixels are, *on average*, colour-compatible with the visible skin, which lowers the suspicion of an obvious colour hole. **What it cannot do:** approve the exposure. A mean over 4 305 pixels says nothing about where those pixels sit, so it cannot show that the scalp is finished, that hairline/crown shading is correct, that ear detail exists, or that any particular region a shorter cut would reveal is presentable. **Spatial inspection and a render-scale visual review remain outstanding, and this figure is not a threshold — no pass/fail tolerance is defined on it.** **(4) THE FINDING THAT CHANGES THE FRAMING:** by envelope the single R2 asset is **as wide as `long` (77.5 vs 78) and lower than `curly`** (lowest y 75.6 vs 61) — a medium-long style with side locks — while C2's default is `short` (width 56, lowest 56). So the 22 students who stored `default`/nothing are **also** not seeing their style: it is **23 of 23 shown a style none of them selected**, one of whom had actively chosen otherwise. It also means **no style is honestly "North Star-equivalent"**, which removes the footing from the cheap fallback option. **(5) OPTIONS COMPARED (qualitative trade-offs only — no time, resource or money estimate was produced):** **A** produce 6 more North Star rasters (art; **D-033 rejects AI for base/rig layers and hair is one** → human paint-over) · **B** re-seat the existing C2 SVGs by wrapper transform (no geometric obstacle found above, but the transform itself is unvalidated per (2c); what it means for the student = flat two-tone SVG hair on the painted figure for 6 of 7 styles, on the **identity** layer — the mixed-stack condition the pilot's §8 gate treated as a defect) · **C** non-produced style → unsupported identity → whole-avatar C2 (D-083 pattern; undermined by (4)) · **D** leave the render alone and stop the UI implying an effect it has not. **(6) RECOMMENDED: D now, A as the real fix, explicitly NOT B** — B is the only option that permanently degrades the painted figure the whole 167A track exists to deliver, and since the geometry does not rule it out, choosing against it is a **product** decision that is recorded rather than hidden: if the owner later prefers coverage over finish, the geometric groundwork is done — the per-style transform validation of (2c) would still be ahead of it. **(7) WHAT IS NOT ESTABLISHED:** geometry is necessary, never sufficient — no render-scale visual review has been done (D-059: never judge fringe on the asset composite); **no transform is validated** (the ~2.8 % value is a candidate only, and the ±8-unit bound is the limit of the numbers because the R2 reference hairline is itself irregular); **the hidden-base finding is an average colour comparison with no spatial inspection**, so it approves no exposure; `long`/`ponytail` drape to y 146/123 onto the torso, which this audit did not measure (R2 shoulder onset y 83.8); the live distribution was RE-MEASURED read-only on 2026-08-20 (28 students, **23 render R2**, within them 20 `(unset)`, 2 `default`, 1 `buzzcut`) and is a point-in-time count, not a standing fact; and **no telemetry, artefact report or user feedback informs any of this** — none exists. **(8) CONFLICT REPORTED, NOT FIXED:** §Current Commit above still says `ad899b6`, which no longer tracks `main` and cannot be kept current by quoting a SHA at all, and the headers of `167a-r2-torso-asset-production-plan.md`, `167a-r2-cosmetic-slot-completion-audit.md` and `167a-r2-torso-a2-art-brief.md` still claim `AVATAR_R2 = false` / `PILOT_WAVE_1_IN_PROGRESS`, superseded by D-101. **(9) BOUNDARIES:** the audit doc, the read-only tool, one `package.json` script, this entry. **No runtime, resolver, manifest, asset, z, transform, test, golden, shop, catalog, database, migration, RLS, Edge Function or flag change; no hairstyle produced, wired or removed; no student record read or written.** **(10) OWNER DECISION (2026-08-20) — D NOW, A LATER, NOT B, NOT C.** The §5 recommendation was accepted as written. **D is implemented**: on a render path that ignores the stored hairstyle the avatar page no longer offers shape controls and states the situation instead — **the UI promise changes, the render does not**. No runtime resolver, manifest, asset, z, transform, golden, database, migration, RLS, Edge Function or flag is touched, and **no student record is written**. **A stays the visual end state and stays blocked** on six hand-painted North Star hair rasters with per-asset promotion + provenance, **D-033 barring AI for base/rig layers and hair being one**; it enters the art queue as itself. **B is declined as a PRODUCT judgement**, recorded rather than hidden because §3 found no geometric obstacle — flat two-tone SVG hair on the painted identity layer for 6 of 7 styles is a worse avatar than today's; if coverage is ever preferred over finish, the unvalidated per-style transform of §3.3 is what remains ahead of it. **C is declined on §3.5** (no style is North Star-equivalent, so it drops nearly everyone to C2 or nominates a style the asset is not). **PRESERVED:** stored hairstyle values are untouched — not cleared, reset or migrated — so they are valid the day A ships; hair **colour** still tints the R2 hair map from `identity.hair_color`; and a path that honours the choice (C2) keeps all seven controls, the active marking and the success toast. **ONE FINDING CORRECTED THE UI WORDING BEFORE IT SHIPPED:** the first proposed message claimed “Din hårfarve virker allerede”, which would have been a SECOND false promise — the tint works, but **no hair-colour picker exists anywhere in the app** (`set_avatar_identity` is called with `p_body_type`/`p_hairstyle`/`p_skin_tone` only, never `p_hair_color`, matching the `js/avatar-layers.js` note that 155E shipped “the data contract only”; a read-only count on 2026-08-20 found **all 28 students with `hair_color` unset**). The text now says plainly that hair colour cannot be chosen yet; **building that picker is 155E's missing UI, not part of D**. **FULLY REVERSIBLE AND SELF-RETIRING:** one capability predicate keyed on the render path actually mounted — when A registers hair assets that honour `identity.hairstyle`, the control returns by itself, with no special cases to unpick and no owner decision embedded in code. (branch `fix/d102-r2-hairstyle-ui-honesty`) (2026-08-17; decided and implemented 2026-08-20) |

| **D-103** | **155E's hair-colour PICKER is built — the section had shipped its data contract and both render paths, but never a control (2026-08-20).** Status **`SECTION_155E_UI_COMPLETE`**. **(1) THE GAP, MEASURED:** 155E locked the palette (8 colours), the `{base, shadow}` token pairs, `hairColorFor`/`hairColorTokensFor`, the `p_hair_color` RPC parameter and BOTH render paths — C2 sets `--hair-base`/`--hair-shadow` on the inline hair SVG, R2 tints the hair map from `tokens.base` — and then stopped, with the code saying so in `js/avatar-layers.js`: *“Render wiring is a later section; 155E ships the data contract only.”* **No page ever called `set_avatar_identity` with `p_hair_color`**, so a read-only count on 2026-08-20 found **all 28 students with `hair_color` unset**, every one of them rendering the default `brown`. The gap was found while auditing the D-102 wording, not by a user report. **(2) NO BACKEND WORK WAS NEEDED, AND NONE WAS DONE:** the RPC already accepts `p_hair_color` and already validates it against exactly the eight palette values, with merge semantics that preserve `body_type`/`hairstyle`/`skin_tone`. **No migration, no RLS, no Edge Function, no schema or data change.** **(3) WHAT SHIPPED:** a `Hårfarve` section in the identity panel, directly under `Frisure` — eight buttons built from the imported `HAIR_COLORS`/`HAIR_COLOR_TOKENS`, so **the control cannot drift from what is painted or from what the database accepts**; each button carries its own token pair as the swatch colour. Reuses the existing `.identity-btn` component; the only new style is a 10 px `.hair-swatch` dot. **(4) SHOWN ON BOTH RENDER PATHS, deliberately unlike D-102's shape control:** the colour is honoured by C2 and by R2, so gating it would repeat the defect D-102 removed rather than avoid it. A test asserts the picker does **not** consult the D-102 capability gate. **(5) DEFENSIVE:** a value outside the palette is refused before the RPC (a rejected write must not look like a saved one, and a UI bug must not surface as a database error); an RPC error toasts and repaints nothing; a missing, empty or unknown stored value resolves through `hairColorFor` so exactly one button is always active. **(6) ONE CONSEQUENCE HANDLED, NOT LEFT:** D-102's panel message said *“Din hårfarve kan endnu ikke vælges.”* Shipping the picker makes that false, so it now reads *“Frisureformer kommer snart til den nye avatar. Farven kan du vælge nedenfor.”* — the D-102 unit test was updated with it. **The shape gap D-102 documents is untouched, and option A is still its fix.** **(7) TESTS:** new `tests/unit/avatar-hair-color-picker.test.mjs` (12) executing the real `renderHairColor` and `handleSetHairColor` from `avatar.html` against a stub DOM and a recording RPC stub — palette order and completeness, the palette pinned to the RPC whitelist, per-button token colours, active marking incl. defaulting from broken identities, a readable Danish label on every button with the swatch `aria-hidden` (colour is never the only cue), save + confirm, only `p_hair_color` sent, invalid value blocked, RPC failure never shown as success, no render-path gate, and the panel wiring. **(8) BOUNDARIES:** `avatar.html` + one new unit test + docs. **No runtime resolver, manifest, asset, z, transform, golden, shop, catalog, database, migration, RLS or Edge Function change; `AVATAR_R2` untouched; no hairstyle produced or wired; no student record written.** (branch `feat/155e-hair-color-picker`) (2026-08-20) |

| **D-104** | **OWNER DECISION — option A is UNBLOCKED: seven hair rasters, and D-042 is extended to new silhouettes (2026-08-21).** Status **`OPTION_A_AUTHORISED — PRODUCTION_NOT_STARTED`**. Six of the seven styles are not in the Master, so they cannot be lifted, and drawing them necessarily changes the silhouette. **The extension therefore removes the one mechanism D-042 relied on for identity-lock — masking — because masking cannot apply to a shape that is not there.** Leaving that hole open would reintroduce R-6 by omission. **(3) THE REPLACEMENT IDENTITY-LOCK — SPLIT AUTHORITY. The gates bound **geometry and finish**; they do **not** detect style drift, style fidelity or identity drift (spec §7). A geometrically perfect `curly` that looks nothing like curly hair passes every automated gate. **(5) SCOPE OF THE CARVE-OUT — HAIR ONLY.** Free AI regeneration or redesign of the avatar, the base, face or eyes remains **forbidden**, as does any unmasked/broad prompt over the base and any change to head size, eye shape, pose, outfit style, skin tone, line-art convention, lighting, palette or proportions (D-032). **Hair silhouette is the single carve-out.** **(6) A CORRECTION TO D-102 AND D-103, which are NOT rewritten:** both state that A needs human paint-over *“because D-033 bars AI for base/rig layers”*. | **D-107** | **`cancelled` becomes a terminal state on `avatar_generation_jobs`; the backlog closure is PREPARED but NOT applied (2026-08-26).** Status **`BACKLOG_CLOSED`** (2026-08-27 — see D-109; was `CLOSURE_PREPARED — RUNTIME_VERIFIED — NOT_APPLIED_TO_PRODUCTION`). `BACKLOG_CLOSED` is deliberately **not** claimed: production is untouched and still reads 21 `failed_permanent` / 22 `failed_retryable` / 2 `pending` / 33 `complete`, with **zero** `cancelled` rows. That status may only be recorded after a verified production migration. **(1) WHAT PROMPTED IT:** the super_admin job table showed **43 failed jobs** and **2 `pending`** that no worker will ever claim — every row initiated April–May 2026, none touched since **2026-05-10**. The queue is not failing; it stopped. **(2) WHY THIS IS A MIGRATION AND NOT AN `UPDATE`, discovered by attempting the `UPDATE` first:** the table is a deliberate state machine with **24 CHECK constraints**, and `cancelled` was rejected by three — `generation_jobs_status_valid` (an explicit six-value allowlist), `generation_jobs_completed_at_consistency` and `generation_jobs_claimed_at_consistency`. The attempt was **refused atomically by the database and changed nothing**. An earlier note in this session that `status` was free text was **wrong** and is corrected here rather than upstream. **(3) THE TWO MODELLING CALLS:** **(a)** `cancelled` is **terminal**, so it joins `complete`/`failed_permanent` in requiring a `completed_at`; rows that already have one keep their original via `coalesce`. **(b)** `cancelled` is the **one status exempt from the `claimed_at` rule**: a job can be cancelled while still queued, and forcing `claimed_at NOT NULL` would make the row assert a claim that never happened. Both alternatives were rejected as **falsifying a record**. **(4) THE BACKFILL IS PINNED TO A SNAPSHOT, BECAUSE A LIVE WRITER EXISTS:** selecting on status alone would mean *whatever is failed or pending at deployment time*. The pg_cron job **`sweep-stale-generation-jobs` is active and runs every 5 minutes** (verified in production) and moves stale `generating` jobs into `failed_retryable` — the very status targeted. The predicate therefore also requires `initiated_at < 2026-06-01`, `resulting_asset_id IS NULL` and all four legacy `generated_glb_*`/`generated_thumbnail_*` shadow columns `IS NULL`; the matching ids are **materialised once** into a temp table, and the `UPDATE` addresses **only those ids** — it carries no status or date predicate of its own, so a job that fails mid-deploy is unreachable by construction. **THREE THINGS CLOSE THE WINDOW, THE FIRST SUFFICIENT ALONE:** a new section 0 takes **`LOCK TABLE … IN ACCESS EXCLUSIVE MODE` explicitly, before anything else**, and holds it until COMMIT, so no sweeper, RPC or Edge Function can interleave — the `ALTER TABLE`s would have taken the same lock, but then the guarantee would have depended on statement order and would silently reopen if the snapshot were ever moved above them; the id set is pinned once; and the cutoff excludes anything newer by construction. **(5) FAIL-CLOSED PRECONDITIONS, ALL `<>` SO BOTH 44 AND 46 ABORT:** total **45**, `failed_permanent` **21**, `failed_retryable` **22**, `pending` **2**, `hard_fail` **0**, `resulting_asset_id` **0**, plus a checked `row_count` of exactly **45** via `GET DIAGNOSTICS` after the `UPDATE`. Any deviation raises and rolls back the whole transaction, **constraint rewrites included**. **(6) A CORRECTION TO THIS ENTRY'S OWN FIRST VERSION:** it claimed *none of the 45 jobs had a generated file, thumbnail or `resulting_asset_id`, so no Storage object is orphaned*. **The file half was wrong.** That check read only the legacy shadow columns; **19 of the 45 carry `generated_files` jsonb** pointing into `avatar-generation-staging` (2 `failed_permanent`, 16 `failed_retryable`, 1 `pending`) — the pipeline produced staging output and failed before promotion. The owner's requested precondition *“ingen genererede filreferencer”* is therefore **unsatisfiable together with 45/21/22/2**, and the conflict was reported rather than resolved silently: the count is **asserted at exactly 19** instead of excluded, which is the stricter guard because a change in either direction aborts. **Nothing is orphaned regardless — this migration deletes nothing, so the references stay on the row.** **(7) HISTORY IS PRESERVED:** the `failed_permanent`/`failed_retryable` distinction exists **only** in `status` — `avatar_generation_events` records `stage`/`outcome`, not job status — so the prior value is captured into `failure_details.cancelled_from` **before** being replaced, merged with `||`. All **1 222** event rows are untouched; the FK is **`ON DELETE RESTRICT`**, and a delete-based cleanup was **rejected** because it would have required destroying **764** audit rows first. **(8) THE APPLICATION STATE MACHINE NOW AGREES:** `GenerationJobStatus` in `supabase/functions/avatar-generation/types.ts` gains `cancelled`, so `GET /status` can return such a row without its declared type being a lie; a test pins the union to the CHECK allowlist **in both directions**. `avatar_ingestion_jobs` is a **separate** state machine on a different table and is deliberately untouched. `claim_generation_job` matches `status='pending'` and `/retry` requires `failed_retryable`, so neither can ever see a cancelled job — no change needed, and a test pins it. **(9) THE RETRY BUTTONS WERE THE ACTUAL HAZARD:** `Retry`/`Process` in `js/admin.js` invoke `avatar-generation` live, so the stale rows were one click from restarting a pipeline the 167A track has moved past. `cancelled` renders as a muted badge and is **excluded from both button predicates**. **(10) THE MIGRATION IS NOW EXECUTED FOR REAL, AND THE EARLIER GAP IS CLOSED:** the previous version of this entry recorded that Docker is absent, that `supabase db reset --local` fails with *“Docker Desktop is a prerequisite for local development”*, and that the SQL had therefore **never been executed anywhere**. It now is. **PGlite** — PostgreSQL compiled to WebAssembly, an ordinary npm devDependency with no daemon, no container, no system PostgreSQL, no network and no external resource — runs the migration file **verbatim** against a throwaway database seeded from `tests/fixtures/avatar-generation-jobs.schema.sql`, a transcription of the live table with **all 24 CHECK constraints**, so the backfill must satisfy every one of them at once. **14 runtime tests** prove: exactly 45 rows cancelled; **five decoys untouched** — including a `failed_retryable` and a `pending` initiated AFTER the cutoff, which is precisely the row the cron sweeper could create mid-deploy; the prior status preserved per row (21/22/2, not flattened); an existing `completed_at` kept and a missing one filled; the two never-claimed jobs still `claimed_at IS NULL`; nothing deleted; the rewritten constraints still rejecting an unknown status, `generating` without `claimed_at`, `complete` without `completed_at`, `pending` WITH `claimed_at` and `cancelled` without `completed_at`; and **six abort paths** (44, 46, right total with wrong 20/23/2 distribution, changed staging-file count, a `hard_fail` verdict, an empty table) each rolling back **the constraint rewrites as well**, verified by re-asserting that the allowlist still refuses `cancelled` after the abort. **LIMITS STATED RATHER THAN GLOSSED:** PGlite is PostgreSQL 18.x and production is 17.6 — every construct used is long stable across both, but it is not the same build; the fixture omits RLS, grants, pg_cron, the events FK and the copyright trigger, none of which this migration can exercise; and **passing is not permission to deploy** — the production run remains outstanding. Both suites were verified non-vacuous against deliberate mutations of the migration. **(10b) SEARCH-PATH HARDENING:** the migration runs as a highly privileged role and calls unqualified functions (`coalesce`, `now`, `jsonb_build_object`, `count`). It now pins **`SET LOCAL search_path = pg_catalog, public, pg_temp`** so nothing can shadow a catalog function and execute as that role, and it addresses the snapshot as **`pg_temp._d107_targets`** so a `public` object of the same name cannot shadow it. `SET LOCAL`, so it reverts at COMMIT and never leaks into the calling session. **(11) BOUNDARIES:** one migration, `GenerationJobStatus`, the `statusBadge` map, one stale invariant in `pipeline-batch-test-plan.md`, two unit-test files, one tracked schema fixture, one test-only devDependency (`@electric-sql/pglite`), this entry. **No Edge Function behaviour, RLS policy, bucket, avatar asset, manifest, z, transform, golden, shop, catalog or student record changed; `AVATAR_R2` untouched; no job re-run; no production mutation of any kind; no AI invoked.** (branch `chore/avatar-jobs-cancelled-status`) (2026-08-26) |
| **D-108** | **The D-107 backfill is bound to the 45 reviewed rows BY IDENTITY, and its resolution is narrowed (2026-08-26).** Status **`IDENTITY_BOUND — APPLIED`** (2026-08-27 — see D-109; was `CLOSURE_PREPARED — IDENTITY_BOUND — NOT_APPLIED_TO_PRODUCTION`). Follows D-107, which is **not rewritten** — the corrections live here, per the register convention. **Production remains untouched: 21 / 22 / 2 / 33, zero `cancelled` rows, no `cancelled_from` trace, and the allowlist still does not contain `cancelled`.** `BACKLOG_CLOSED` is still **not** claimed. **(1) THE ROOT CAUSE:** every D-107 precondition described the **SHAPE** of the backlog — 45 total, 21/22/2 by status, 19 with staging file references, no `hard_fail`, no promoted asset, the cutoff, the empty legacy shadow columns — and **shape is not identity**. Any other 45 rows with those same properties would have satisfied all of them. Since the migration had already been merged to `main` but **not applied**, it was still correctable in place; no second production migration was created. **(2) THE FIX — A FINGERPRINT OVER THE SORTED IDS:** the DO block now computes `encode(sha256(convert_to(string_agg(t.id::text, ',' order by t.id), 'UTF8')), 'hex')` over `pg_temp._d107_targets` and aborts with **`D107_PRECONDITION_TARGET_FINGERPRINT`** unless it equals a constant measured read-only against production on 2026-08-26. **WHY EACH CHOICE IS FORCED:** the ids come from the snapshot taken **under the explicit lock**, so the hash describes exactly the rows the `UPDATE` will touch; `ORDER BY id` uses uuid's own btree comparison, a byte compare of the 16-byte value, which is locale-independent — **ordering by `id::text` was rejected** because text ordering follows the database collation and a punctuation-insensitive collation would reorder the hyphenated form; `id::text` renders the canonical lowercase form, so every element is fixed-width; `','` separates them so no two different sets can concatenate to the same string; `convert_to(…, 'UTF8')` pins the bytes hashed. Verified deterministic by computing it twice in one statement and comparing, over **45 distinct** ids with input length **1664 = 45×36 + 44**. **(3) THE HASH IS ALL THAT IS STORED:** the 45 raw uuids are **not committed to this repository, not logged and not printed** — a test asserts that the migration contains no uuid-shaped string at all. **(4) FAIL-CLOSED DETAIL:** the comparison is `IS DISTINCT FROM`, not `<>` — an empty snapshot makes `string_agg` NULL and the digest NULL, and `NULL <> text` is NULL, which would **not** fire the branch. **(5) ALL D-107 COUNT GUARDS ARE KEPT** as defence in depth and run **before** the fingerprint, because when the backlog has genuinely changed they name *what* changed, while the fingerprint can only say *not the reviewed set*. **(6) SEARCH PATH NARROWED:** `pg_catalog, public, pg_temp` → **`pg_catalog, pg_temp`**. Every relation was verified schema-qualified first (`public.avatar_generation_jobs`, `pg_temp._d107_targets`; the only bare name is the `CREATE TEMPORARY TABLE` target, which always lands in `pg_temp`), and every unqualified function call resolves from `pg_catalog`. A test now fails if `public` returns or if any relation is left unqualified. **MEASURED, READ-ONLY EVIDENCE — NOT A GRANT CHANGE:** only **`postgres`** and **`supabase_admin`** hold `CREATE` on schema `public`; `anon`, `authenticated`, `service_role`, `authenticator`, `supabase_auth_admin` and `supabase_storage_admin` have `USAGE` only. So this is defence in depth against a privileged mistake, not a response to a reachable hostile object. **No grant was altered.** **(7) PGLITE PINNED:** `^0.5.7` → exactly **`0.5.7`** via `--save-exact`, consistent across `package.json`, `package-lock.json` and the historically tracked `node_modules/.package-lock.json`. A caret on the harness that supplies the runtime evidence would let the evidence change without the repository changing. No other package was installed or upgraded, and the historically tracked `node_modules` files were deliberately **not** cleaned up here. **(8) THE ONE PLACE THE TEST HARNESS EDITS THE MIGRATION, DISCLOSED RATHER THAN HIDDEN:** synthetic fixtures cannot carry the real ids — that is the point of the hash — so for fixture-based scenarios `migrationFor()` substitutes that **single 64-character constant** with the fixture's own fingerprint, asserting it occurs **exactly once** and that the result actually changed. **In those scenarios the SQL executed is NOT byte-identical to what will deploy.** Two tests compensate and both pass: the **unmodified** file is executed against a synthetic backlog whose counts match the reviewed one exactly and must abort on `D107_PRECONDITION_TARGET_FINGERPRINT`, proving the gate is live in the committed file; and the on-disk constant is asserted to still be the production fingerprint, so no test can weaken what ships. **(9) EVIDENCE:** 35 source assertions + 17 runtime tests, all green. The decisive new one swaps **one** target id while total, per-status distribution, staging-file count, `hard_fail` and assets stay identical — every other guard passes and the migration aborts on identity alone. **Counterfactual mutation testing:** removing the fingerprint guard, weakening it to `<>`, reintroducing `public`, ordering by `::text`, unqualifying the production table and blanking the expected constant are **each** caught by the source suite; removing the guard additionally fails the runtime identity test. **(10) STILL OUTSTANDING, UNCHANGED:** PGlite is PostgreSQL 18.x and production is 17.6; the fixture omits RLS, grants, pg_cron, the events FK and the copyright trigger, none of which this migration can exercise; and **the migration has still never been executed against production**. The fingerprint is valid only while the backlog is untouched — if any of the 45 rows changes before `supabase db push`, the migration will correctly refuse, and the constant must be re-measured rather than relaxed. **(11) BOUNDARIES:** the existing migration file, the two unit-test suites, `package.json`/`package-lock.json`/`node_modules/.package-lock.json`, this entry. **No new migration; no `supabase db push`; no production write, DDL or migration; read-only production queries only; no grant, RLS, bucket, Storage object, Edge Function, avatar asset or student record touched; no avatar job restarted; no Supabase branch or external resource created.** (branch `chore/d107-target-fingerprint`) (2026-08-26) |

Registered as **`20260827125327 = avatar_jobs_cancelled_status`**. **(2) HOW, AND WHY NOT `db push` — PATH D:** a read-only migration preflight found the CLI **refuses to run at all**: `supabase db push --linked --dry-run` exits **1** with *“Remote migration versions not found in local migrations directory”* and suggests `migration repair --status reverted 20260823144030`. **The root cause is structural, not a one-off:** this project has **never** used `db push` against production. All 108 pre-existing remote records were created through the dashboard/Management API — `created_by` is the owner's e-mail on every one — with server-generated timestamps, while repository files carry hand-written ones. The intent was “no decorative border”; the model read it as an instruction to make the artwork fill the frame, and **obeyed it**. Every candidate is 1,6–2,1× too large for the head — `tousled` 2,12×, `ponytail` 2,11×, `short` 2,03×, `long` 1,80×, `buzz` 1,74×, `afro` 1,67×, `curly` 1,63× — so the silhouette overruns its C2 envelope and reaches below the neck limit. **This is one method error, not seven artistic failures**, and it was initially misdiagnosed as “image models ignore pixel coordinates” before the prompt was re-read. **(4) THE GEOMETRY WAS NOT REPAIRED, DELIBERATELY:** a per-style scale-and-translate would have turned two gates green, but each style needs a different factor derived from the target, which is fitting the artwork to the measurement rather than authoring it to the geometry. It would also have halved the line weight relative to the figure — a finish defect **no gate can see** — and resampling would have made `alpha-clean-no-halo` worse, not better. **A candidate that fails stays classified as failed.** **(5) WHAT DID WORK, AND IT IS THE EXPENSIVE HALF:** all seven pass `dimensions`, `has-ink`, `covers-the-crown`, `clears-the-eye-line` and `legible-at-render-sizes`, and all seven pass **`luminance-map` with mean saturation 0,0000** — the requirement §5.1 calls the single easiest way to waste a completed asset. Transparency, canvas and the greyscale contract were correct on the first attempt. **(6) THE OWNER VERDICT — THE ROW D-105 MADE LOAD-BEARING:** reviewed on a purpose-built page showing each candidate at the four real render sizes (180×270, 112×168, 72×108, 52×78) with the default `brown` token, a tint strip across all eight 155E tokens, the C2 silhouette as shape authority, and — for `long` and `ponytail` — the **D-106 mandatory composed review with `armor-knight-r2-v1` equipped**. **The owner accepts the EXPRESSION AND STYLE DIRECTION on all seven** — line, value structure, and whether each silhouette reads as its style. That is the whole of the verdict. **It does NOT approve correct fit, correct size, technical validity, promotion or runtime use, and it does NOT settle identity preservation**, because identity can only be judged once the hair sits at the right size on the head — which these candidates do not. The D-105 owner row is therefore **partly** discharged: the style-fidelity half is answered, the identity half is not. **The seven oversized, failed candidates count as evidence for the style direction and for nothing else.** **(7) WHAT THE VERDICT DOES NOT SETTLE, STATED SO IT IS NOT OVERREAD:** the candidates were shown mounted at 1,6–2,1× the correct scale, so what was judged is the artwork's **character** — line, value structure, whether each silhouette reads as its style. **Fit was not approved and could not be**; it looks different once the scale is right, and that judgement belongs to a later round. The **`long` and `ponytail` composed views were seen and called visually fine in this style review, but they CANNOT approve the final fit over shoulders and armour**, because the hair was shown 1,6–2,1× too large — which is precisely the interaction D-106 made a separate mandatory condition. **No candidate is promotable.** **(8) WHAT CARRIES FORWARD:** the style language, the North Star finish wording and the greyscale requirement are validated and are reused verbatim. Only the framing instruction is to change — the “fill the canvas” line is removed and pixel coordinates are replaced by proportional framing, which image models follow far better. **(9) EVIDENCE:** seven candidates with SHA-256, seven prompts, seven raw API responses, eleven-gate reports per style, and both manifests are kept **gitignored** under `tools/avatar/build/r2-hair-first-slice/20260828-131348/` (41 files, 12,3 MB) — per the D-088 convention that non-reproducible generation inputs live outside git.
