| **D-112** | **Deterministic orphan-dust removal is ACCEPTED as a pre-review step, the alpha gate's scale contract is CORRECTED, and the tool's write path is hardened (2026-08-29).** Status **`ALPHA_CLEANUP_ACCEPTED — SCALE_CONTRACT_SUPERSEDED_BY_D-115 — TOOL_ONLY — NO_CANDIDATE_PROMOTED`** (2026-08-30 — see D-115; was `ALPHA_CLEANUP_ACCEPTED — TOOL_ONLY — NO_CANDIDATE_PROMOTED`). Successor to D-111, which is **not rewritten**, per the register convention. **§(3) below describes the alpha gate as checking the authoring canvas AND the bare downscale. That was true when written; D-115 moved the visual gates onto the decoded runtime asset and left the numbers alone. The body here is deliberately not edited.** This is the entry PR #213 deliberately left out because D-111 was still unmerged and a new D-number would have collided. **(1) THE DECISION:** a generated candidate carries thousands of isolated pixels at 1–3 % opacity — encoder dust, not artwork. Measured on the afro: **5 938** orphan-soft pixels at authoring scale, mean alpha **2,4/255**, 97 % of them at alpha ≤ 8. They are invisible, and they were the only thing between a geometrically correct candidate and the alpha gate. `tools/avatar/clean-r2-hair-alpha.mjs` removes them with **no AI, no network and no randomness**. A pixel is cleared **only if BOTH hold on the ORIGINAL input**: `alpha < 24`, and it is orphan-soft by the gate's own definition (`0 < alpha < 128`, no ink among the four orthogonal neighbours). Every other byte is copied unchanged, and decisions are read from a snapshot, so clearing one pixel can never orphan its neighbour in the same run — **no cascade, no dependence on scan order**. **(2) WHY 24, AND WHY IT WAS NOT TUNED HERE:** `ALPHA_FLOOR = 24` is the project's existing threshold from `openai-generate-torso-item.mjs` (*“below this, a pixel is background glow rather than artwork”*). It was chosen for the torso work **before any hair candidate existed** and is reused unchanged; a threshold fitted to make one asset pass proves nothing about the next. **(3) A REAL GATE DEFECT WAS FOUND AND CORRECTED, NOT WORKED AROUND:** `check-r2-hair-candidate.mjs` applied `HALO_TOLERANCE = 16` — the **served** constant from `promote-r2-torso-asset` — at **authoring** resolution, while its comment claimed it followed the torso convention. That is four times stricter than the torso gate at the same resolution. Both budgets are now declared and both are checked: **64 at 1024×1536** and **16 after `downscaleHalf()`** at 512×768, the pipeline's own function reused rather than reimplemented. `gates()` now **requires** the served number; a default would let a caller skip half the contract in silence. **The tolerances were corrected to the torso convention, not raised to fit the afro.** **(4) FOUR SECURITY DEFECTS IN THE FIRST IMPLEMENTATION, FOUND BY REVIEW AND CLOSED BEFORE MERGE (PR #213):** **(a) the served half of the gate was not being measured at all** — the test helper called `downscaleHalf(w, h, rgba)` and then read `.rgba`, `.w` and `.h` off the result, but that function returns a **raw RGBA `Buffer`**; the three properties were `undefined`, the loop ran `y < undefined` zero times, and every served assertion reported **0 orphans regardless of input**. A gate that could not fail — the exact defect the suite exists to prevent. It was caught because 0 contradicted a measured 1 292 on the real afro. **(b)** `isOrphanSoft` / `countOrphanSoft` existed in **two** copies, so the threshold deciding what may be removed and the threshold deciding whether the result is acceptable could drift apart; they are now exported from the gate and imported by the cleanup tool. **(c)** the write contract was a **blacklist**, which permitted `docs/`, `index.html`, `package.json` and any path outside the repository entirely; it is now a **positive allowlist** — `tools/avatar/build/alpha-cleanup/` and nowhere else, for the PNG and its sidecar alike. **(d)** the tool could write before validating; it is now **fail-closed** — encode, decode back, compare, then **nine postconditions**, all before the first byte reaches disk, so a sidecar carrying `"pass": true` cannot exist unless all nine ran and held. **(5) TWO FURTHER DEFECTS CLOSED IN THIS ENTRY'S OWN PR:** **(a) THE ALLOWLIST WAS LEXICAL.** `resolve()` + `relative()` answer a question about strings; the write does not. `tools/avatar/build/` is gitignored scratch that tools create and delete freely, so a symlink planted inside it satisfied the string check and still redirected the bytes anywhere on disk. The **dangling** case was sharper: `existsSync()` follows the link, finds nothing and reports **false**, so the “already exists” guard waved through precisely the plant that escapes. The check now resolves through symlinks on the real filesystem, the presence guards use `lstat`, and both files are created with **O_EXCL**, which refuses to follow a link at the final component at all. **One subtlety was found only by writing the test:** comparing `realpath(path)` against `realpath(writeRoot)` is **self-defeating** — if the write root is itself the link, both sides resolve to the attacker's directory, the two agree, and the escape is accepted exactly when it succeeded. The physical check is therefore anchored on the **repository root**, with the write root re-derived from it lexically. **(b) THE COMMIT WAS NOT ATOMIC.** Two unsynchronised `writeFileSync` calls meant a crash between them left a **PNG with no report** — an artefact that looks like a validated output while carrying no evidence that a single postcondition ran, and `writeFileSync` itself can leave a truncated file at the final path. Both files are now staged under unguessable temporary names in the destination directory, `fsync`ed, and renamed into place with the **SIDECAR RENAMED FIRST**. Two files cannot be published in one atomic step, so the ordering is the guarantee: of the two possible crash residues only the harmless one is reachable — a report with no image, which is obviously incomplete and makes the next run refuse. **(6) EVIDENCE:** **78 unit tests across four suites, all green** — `avatar-r2-hair-candidate-check` 17, `avatar-r2-hair-alpha-cleanup` 23, `avatar-r2-hair-alpha-guards` 21, `avatar-r2-hair-alpha-write-safety` 17. They include an end-to-end case where **17 served orphans fail the gate and 16 pass** while the authoring budget stays satisfied, so the served bound is proven to be what decides the outcome; each of the nine postconditions; the allowlist in both directions; and three counterfactual mutations. On the hash-locked afro: **5 938 → 27** authoring, **1 292 → 11** served, **5 911** pixels cleared in 255 components, highest removed alpha **23**, geometry identical, **9/9** postconditions, **11/11** gates. Input `14a037a0…` unchanged; output `0dacc5ce…` is **byte-identical across all three tool versions**, so neither the security hardening nor the new commit path moved the result. **(7) HONEST LIMITS:** creating a symlink needs a privilege the development machine does not have (EPERM), so the three real-filesystem link tests **run in CI on Linux and are skipped locally**; the policy itself is exercised everywhere through an injected `realpath`. The commit ORDER is asserted at source level, because no runtime test can observe a crash between two renames. The guard suite **failed its first CI run** with `ENOENT` on `mkdtemp` — `tools/avatar/build/` is gitignored and absent in a fresh checkout, and the suite had passed locally only because the directory happened to be lying around; fixed and verified by removing the directory and re-running. Full local CI was **not** achieved: `@electric-sql/pglite` is not installed in either clone and the two D-094 binary-dependent torso suites need the vendored Windows libwebp. **(8) CLEANUP IS NOT APPROVAL:** it removes a measurement obstacle and nothing else. Every geometry and identity requirement in D-102/D-104 still applies unchanged, per-asset owner sign-off at real render scale (D-059, D-105) remains the only place style fidelity and identity are judged, and the D-106 composed review for `long` and `ponytail` is untouched. A cleaned candidate that passes all eleven gates is **a candidate for review, never a promoted asset**. The owner's D-111 verdict is unchanged and is still style-direction only. **(9) BOUNDARIES:** two tools, four unit suites, `docs/167a-r2-hair-a-production-spec.md` and this entry. **No candidate promoted, registered, activated or wired; no image request of any kind; no hairstyle generated or edited; `hairSrcForR2`, `R2_MANIFEST`, `AVATAR_R2`, every runtime file, asset, manifest, z and transform table, golden baseline, shop, catalog and feature flag untouched; no Supabase data, Storage, policy, migration, RLS or Edge Function touched; no student record read or written; the afro source byte-identical throughout; all generated artefacts remain gitignored under `tools/avatar/build/`.** (branch `fix/alpha-cleanup-symlink-atomicity`) (2026-08-29) |

| **D-114** | **Option A LANDS for one style: `hairSrcForR2` is now style-aware, the afro is registered, and the note stays (2026-08-29).** Status **`OPTION_A_PARTIALLY_LANDED — AFRO_ONLY — UI_NOTE_RETAINED`**. Successor to D-102 and D-113, which are **not rewritten** — only D-102's status line is re-pointed here, per the register convention. D-102 §(1) described `hairSrcForR2` as accepting `identity` and never reading it; that was true when written and is superseded by this entry. **(1) WHAT CHANGED, IN ONE FUNCTION:** `hairSrcForR2(identity)` now resolves the identity's `hairstyle` to a registered R2 asset, and falls back to **`northstar`** when that style has none. `R2_MANIFEST.hair` becomes `{ "northstar": 1, "afro": 1 }`, and `assets/avatar-r2/hair/hair-afro-v1.webp` (36 356 B, `675f8f951c65266c75cc661163219a7958b612b0d45ec9471655f0bebf9eb09a`) is the first per-style R2 hair asset the project has ever had. **(2) WHY THE FALLBACK IS `northstar` AND NOT `null` — THE DECISION THAT MATTERS MOST HERE:** hair is a **mandatory** layer, so a null return drops the WHOLE avatar to C2 via `r2StackSrcsFor`. `"northstar"` is not a hairstyle value; it is the generic hair every R2 render used before per-style artwork existed. Resolving strictly, with C2 fallback for un-produced styles, would therefore have taken **every student whose hairstyle is not `afro` off the R2 path entirely** — a rollback of D-101's default-on decision delivered by a one-line resolver change, in production, silently. The owner was shown that consequence and chose the staged path: keep today's render exactly as it is for un-produced styles, and change the picture only for the style that actually has artwork. **Strict C2 fallback remains the honest END state and is a separate decision, correct once all seven styles exist — not before.** **(3) MEASURED, NOT ASSUMED:** the resolver was exercised over 22 inputs — `afro` → its own asset; all seven C2 styles, all four 155F legacy aliases, `constructor`, `__proto__`, `toString`, the empty string, a number, `null`, `undefined`, and a non-object identity → `northstar`, i.e. **byte-identical behaviour to before this change** for every student except one with a stored `afro`. The lookup uses `hasOwnProperty` rather than a bare index, because `hairstyle` is database data and `"constructor"` would otherwise resolve to an inherited property instead of a registered asset. **(4) THE ASSET CAME OFF THE RUNTIME PATH, NOT AN IMPROVISED ONE:** owner-approved PNG → `downscaleHalf` ÷2 → 512×768 RGBA reference → `cwebp -lossless -exact -z 9 -metadata none`, the D-061 runtime-asset settings, with the pinned libwebp 1.5.0 binaries (`6fcb8098…` / `ee66951d…`) verified by SHA before use. `encode-webp.mjs --half` was deliberately NOT used: it is lossy and lets cwebp do the resize, which makes "the asset matches what cwebp produced" circular and would move the served pixels the alpha gate was measured on. The decoded asset equals its reference **byte for byte** (0 differing bytes), the encode is deterministic across two runs, and orphan-soft on the decoded asset is **11** against the served limit of 16. The reference is the same file the owner reviewed at the four render sizes, so **the approval covers the exact pixels this asset carries**, not an approximation. **(5) TWO SENTINELS FIRED, EXACTLY AS THEY WERE BUILT TO:** `avatar-r2-hair-fit-measure` asserted that `hairSrcForR2` still ignored its identity, with the message *"the R2 hair gap may be closed; update D-102 and this test"*; `avatar-hairstyle-capability` pinned `Object.keys(R2_MANIFEST.hair) === ["northstar"]`. Both now guard the NEW contract, and both were rewritten to measure BEHAVIOUR rather than source text — the audit's own lesson. New guards replace them: the per-style resolution, the northstar fallback across ten styles, the inherited-property cases, that northstar stays registered, and a tripwire that fires when all seven styles exist so the fallback and the note can retire together. **(6) A CLAIM IN THE OLD SENTINEL WAS WRONG, AND IS CORRECTED:** its title said the capability predicate "tracks the manifest, so option A re-enables the UI by itself". It does not. `hairstyleShapesSupported()` returns `activeRenderPath !== "r2"` and never reads the manifest, so producing artwork re-enables nothing. **(7) THE UI IS UNCHANGED BY OWNER DECISION:** the D-102 note stays and the hairstyle buttons stay hidden on the R2 path. Six of seven styles still have no R2 asset, so offering seven buttons there would present six choices that do nothing — less honest than the note, not more. **The practical consequence, stated plainly: the afro renders for a student whose stored hairstyle is already `afro`, and it cannot be SELECTED from the R2 path until the UI decision is revisited.** That revisit belongs with the remaining six assets. **(8) WHAT THIS DOES NOT SETTLE:** the six unproduced styles, the strict-C2-fallback end state, the D-102 UI decision, and the staleness of [`167a-r2-hair-identity-audit.md`](./167a-r2-hair-identity-audit.md), whose §1 quotes the old resolver body — its status line is re-pointed here, the measurements in it stand, and re-deriving the document is a separate task. **(9) EVIDENCE:** 605 unit tests, 600 pass, 4 skipped, 1 failing — the pre-existing `@electric-sql/pglite` gap, unrelated. No golden baseline moved, because no test identity has a stored `afro`. **(10) BOUNDARIES:** one resolver, one manifest entry, one new asset, two test files and this entry. **No hairstyle produced or generated; no image request; `AVATAR_R2`, the z and transform tables, the picker, the shop, the catalog, every other asset and every feature flag untouched; no Supabase data, Storage, policy, migration, RLS or Edge Function touched; no student record read or written; the six unproduced styles render exactly as they did before.** (branch `feat/r2-hair-afro-style-aware`) (2026-08-29) |
| **D-115** | **OWNER DECISION — the visual acceptance gates measure the FINISHED, DECODED RUNTIME ASSET, and `no-floating-islands` uses 8-neighbour connectivity (2026-08-30).** Status **`RUNTIME_GATE_CONTRACT_ACTIVE — GENERAL_RULE — SHORT_REJECTED_BY_D-116`** (2026-08-30 — see D-116; was `RUNTIME_GATE_CONTRACT_ACTIVE — GENERAL_RULE — SHORT_NOT_PROMOTED`). **§(8) records the `short` candidate at 11/11. That was true of the gate set as it then stood; D-116 added the missing top bound and the same candidate is now 10/11 and REJECTED. The body here is deliberately not edited.** Successor to D-112 (whose scale contract this revises) and to D-102 §4A; neither body is rewritten — only D-112's status line is re-pointed here, per the register convention. **(1) TWO DECISIONS, IMPLEMENTED AS ONE GENERAL CONTRACT.** *(a)* The visual acceptance gates now measure the finished 512×768 asset **after the whole existing pipeline** — authoring alpha cleanup → `downscaleHalf` → served alpha cleanup → `cwebp -lossless -exact -z 9 -metadata none` → decode with the pinned `dwebp`. *(b)* `no-floating-islands` examines **all eight** neighbour positions instead of four. Both are rules for **every** R2 hair asset. **There is no `short`-specific exception, and none was created.** **(2) THE CHECKS SPLIT IN TWO, AND THE OUTPUT SAYS WHICH IS WHICH.** **AUTHORING PRECONDITIONS (2)** — `dimensions`, `luminance-map` — are properties of the delivered source: the right canvas, and a greyscale value map. They stay on the 1024×1536 candidate because no downscale, cleanup or lossless encode can create or repair them. **RUNTIME ACCEPTANCE GATES (9)** — `has-ink`, `covers-the-crown`, `clears-the-eye-line`, `respects-the-neck`, `within-style-envelope`, `centred-on-the-skull`, `no-floating-islands`, `alpha-clean-no-halo`, `legible-at-render-sizes` — are the visual judgement and measure the decoded asset. 2 + 9 = the same eleven named checks as before, re-homed rather than added to. The decoded WebP is verified **byte for byte** against the reference the encoder was handed; if that fails, no gate result means anything and the run is disqualified. **(3) WHY RUNTIME PIXELS ARE THE ACCEPTANCE BASIS.** Judging an intermediate is judging something that never ships. It is the D-059 arm-fringe lesson again: the full-res composite flattered the matte, and the defect existed only at render scale on the real surface. The downscale and the two cleanup passes are not cosmetic — they merge pixels, remove dust, and change every count the gates read. **Measured on the Short candidate, the two images genuinely disagree:** the authoring canvas reaches x **112.03**, outside `short`'s envelope of 108 ± 4, while the shipped asset reaches **111.5625** and is inside it. A candidate can legitimately fail on a canvas nobody will ever see and be correct in the file that ships. Only one of the two is the product. **(4) WHY 8-NEIGHBOUR MATCHES VISIBLE RASTER CONNECTION.** 4-neighbour connectivity sees only the four sides, so two pixels sharing a **corner** are separate to the algorithm while being physically joined on screen — there is no gap between them to see. After a ÷2 box downscale that is routine, not exotic: averaging 2×2 blocks regularly leaves a silhouette's outermost pixel attached only at a corner. **The concrete case:** on Short's served asset the pixel at **(260,30)** has alpha exactly **128** — ink by one level — and its four orthogonal neighbours are soft (49, 65, 108) or empty (0), while its down-left diagonal is ink at alpha **225**. It touches the hair mass. Calling it a floating island reports a defect no student can see, and the only ways to 'fix' it would be to edit the artwork or loosen a threshold — both worse than counting the corner the eye already counts. **It also ends an inconsistency:** every other component algorithm in the R2 asset contract was already 8-neighbour (`check-r2-torso-candidate.mjs`, `promote-r2-torso-asset.mjs`'s own `no-floating-islands`, `build-r2-torso-occlusion-mask.mjs`, the torso item generator's backfill check). The hair gate was the single outlier and nothing recorded why. **(5) THE HONEST CONSEQUENCE FOR DIAGONAL CHAINS — STATED, NOT BURIED.** Under 8-neighbour an **unbroken diagonal chain** of ink pixels counts as connected, however long and however thin: a one-pixel-wide staircase running away from the hair mass would be judged part of it. That is a real widening of what 'one component' admits, it is not hypothetical, and it is **accepted deliberately** — such a chain is continuous ink on screen, and no gate here has ever claimed to judge whether a shape is good hair. That remains the owner sign-off D-105 made load-bearing. What 8-neighbour still refuses is exactly what it is for: ink separated by **at least one whole pixel** of non-ink. A test pins both halves — a 12-link diagonal chain reads as one component, and breaking a single link immediately separates the tail. **(6) NOTHING WAS LOOSENED.** The ink threshold (**128**), `ALPHA_FLOOR` (**24**), the orphan budget (**16**), the authoring halo budget (**64**), `MAX_SPECK_PX` (**16**), the envelope tolerance (**±4**), every per-style envelope, both cleanup algorithms, the generator, the prompt and the hairstyle geometry are byte-for-byte what they were. **What changed is which image the unchanged numbers are read from.** Two consequential details: the 64-px authoring halo budget is no longer re-asserted by the gate tool — it is enforced where it always actually bit, in `clean-r2-hair-alpha.mjs`'s `authoringWithinBudget` postcondition, which refuses to *write* above it — and the orphan-soft definition itself deliberately **stays at four orthogonal neighbours**, because it decides what the cleanup tools may delete. Widening the dust rule would licence removing more artwork; widening the island rule only regroups pixels and removes nothing. Two different questions that share the word 'neighbour'; only one moved. **(7) THE AFRO IS UNCHANGED, AND A HANDOVER FIGURE IS CORRECTED.** `hair-afro-v1.webp` (36 356 B, `675f8f95…`) and both tracked candidate fixtures are byte-for-byte untouched, pinned by a CI-safe test. On its own decoded pixels the shipped afro passes **9/9** runtime gates, with envelope **30.625…129.0625** and orphan-soft **11** against the limit of 16. A handover note attributed **orphan-soft 2** to that SHA; re-measurement shows **2 belongs to a different object** — the afro re-run through today's full pipeline, which now includes the served cleanup pass that did not exist when the afro was promoted. The shipped bytes decode to the pre-cleanup buffer (verified true) and not to the post-cleanup one (verified false). **The asset is deliberately NOT rebuilt: the owner approved these pixels (D-114), and rebuilding would change its SHA.** The afro was also one component under the OLD 4-neighbour rule, so D-115 cannot be what makes it pass — the change is not a rule fitted to a result. **(8) THE SHORT RESULT — PROVEN, NOT ASSUMED.** The `short` candidate (`de930f9b…` luminance, from raw `c7a02cbd…`) passes **11/11**: 2 authoring preconditions + 9 runtime gates. Runtime envelope **50.3125…111.5625**, width **61.25**, orphan-soft **14** ≤ 16, one component. The decoded WebP equals its reference byte for byte and the encode is deterministic across runs. Counterfactuals prove each half is load-bearing: reverting the SAME asset to 4-neighbour yields **two** components and one speck and fails the gate, and measuring the authoring intermediate flips `within-style-envelope` to a failure. **Short is NOT promoted.** It stays in gitignored scratch; `assets/avatar-r2/hair/` is unchanged, and owner visual sign-off at real render scale (D-059/D-105) is the only thing that can start promotion. **(9) WHERE IT LANDED IN CODE.** `check-r2-hair-candidate.mjs` v2.0.0 splits `gates()` into `authoringPreconditions()` and `runtimeGates()`, exports `countComponents` and `NEIGHBOURS_8`, and its `run()` now drives the whole pipeline. New `build-r2-hair-runtime-asset.mjs` owns that pipeline, reusing `servedPass` and the torso path's `CWEBP_ARGS` and SHA-pinned binaries rather than copying them, and writing only into gitignored scratch. `clean-r2-hair-alpha.mjs` v4.0.0 replaces the `servedWithinBudget` postcondition — which measured the bare downscale, an intermediate — with `runtimeWithinBudget` on the buffer that ships; the intermediate count is still reported. `clean-served-alpha.mjs` v2.0.0 takes the same vocabulary, and the header that recorded this decision as *'not this tool's to take'* now records that it was taken. A repository-wide search confirmed the hair gate held the **only** 4-neighbour component rule in this contract; `build-hair-clean.mjs`'s 4-neighbour fill is Master segmentation for the paused Gate-3 work, a different question, and is untouched. **(10) EVIDENCE:** 655 unit tests — 650 pass, 4 skipped, **1 failing: the pre-existing `@electric-sql/pglite` module gap, unrelated to this change and present on `main`**. 30 gate tests, 16 runtime-asset tests (locally; the file is binary-dependent and listed in `tests/unit-ci-exclusions.mjs` with its justification — every structural claim is duplicated on synthetic fixtures in two suites that DO run in CI, so CI coverage did not shrink). `build:cloudflare` green. No golden baseline moved. **(11) BOUNDARIES:** two tools revised, one tool added, five test files, the CI exclusion list and documentation. **Zero image requests. No promotion, no manifest, resolver, z or transform change, no `AVATAR_R2` change, no asset added to or removed from `assets/avatar-r2/`, no prompt change, no geometric edit, no threshold or envelope change, no candidate deleted or moved, no Supabase, Storage, migration, RLS or Edge Function touched, no student record read or written.** Every student's avatar renders exactly as it did before. (branch `feat/r2-hair-runtime-gates-8-connectivity`) (2026-08-30) |
| **D-116** | **OWNER DECISION — the acceptance gates bound the TOP of a hairstyle, and the style set stays at seven (2026-08-30).** Status **`TOP_BOUND_ACTIVE — STYLE_SET_UNCHANGED — SHORT_REJECTED — EYE_GAP_CLOSED_BY_D-118`** (2026-08-31 — see D-118; was `TOP_BOUND_ACTIVE — STYLE_SET_UNCHANGED — SHORT_REJECTED`). Successor to D-115 and to the D-102/D-104 geometry targets; no earlier body is rewritten — only D-115's status line is re-pointed here, per the register convention. **(1) THE GAP, STATED PLAINLY:** nothing bounded how HIGH a hairstyle could reach. `covers-the-crown` is a **minimum** (the hair must reach at least the bald skull at y 31.6); `respects-the-neck` bounds the **bottom**; and `within-style-envelope` — despite its name — bounded only **x**. A candidate could tower arbitrarily far over its style and pass every one of the eleven checks. This was not theoretical: the `short` candidate matched short's width almost exactly (50.3…111.6 against 52…108 ± 4) while standing **11 units taller than short has any business standing**, and scored 11/11. **(2) HOW IT SURFACED — THREE INDEPENDENT ROUTES TO THE SAME ANSWER.** A silhouette overlay of the candidate against the C2 `short` path; a measurement (candidate crown **9.38** against short's own **20.50**, with the vertical profile sitting between `tousled` 13.00 and `curly` 14.08); and the owner's own concept sheet, on which the candidate visibly resembles the entry labelled **Spiky**, not the one labelled **Short**. The gates agreed with none of them, because they could not see the axis in question. **(3) THE FIX:** `STYLE_TARGETS` gains **`highestY`** per style, and `within-style-envelope` now bounds both axes — `xLo/xHi` as before, plus `envelope.yLo >= highestY - Y_TOLERANCE`. The gate's name always promised an envelope and delivered an x-span; it now delivers an envelope. **The BOTTOM deliberately stays with `respects-the-neck`**, which already owns it and knows about draping styles: this gate owns width and crown height, that one owns collision with the D-090 garment. Two questions, two gates, no overlap. The check count is unchanged at eleven — 2 authoring preconditions + 9 runtime gates. **(4) THE NUMBERS ARE THE ARTWORK'S OWN, AND THAT IS PROVEN RATHER THAN ASSERTED.** `highestY` is measured by `measure-r2-hair-fit.measureC2Style` — the **same tool, same scan, same exact quadratic-Bezier and line column crossings** that produced `xLo`, `xHi` and `lowestY`. It is literally the mirror of the existing `lowestY` line: `Math.min` of the span tops where that takes `Math.max` of the span bottoms. The top had simply never been computed. A test re-derives **all four** numbers for **all seven** styles from the tool and requires them to equal the table — and the pre-existing three reproduce **exactly**, which is what establishes that the new one was measured the same way. Measured crowns: `buzz` 21.50, `short` 20.50, `ponytail` 19.50, `long` 18.50, `curly` 14.08, `tousled` 13.00, `afro` 6.02. **(5) THE TOLERANCE:** **`Y_TOLERANCE = 4`**, stated as its own exported constant rather than aliased to `X_TOLERANCE`, because the two bound different things and should be free to diverge if a measurement ever justifies it. The value is 4 for the same reason X is: it catches a wrong silhouette, not an artistic difference. It was checked against the only two R2 rasters that exist — the **approved afro clears it by 3.6 units** (crown 5.6 against a limit of 2.02) and the **`short` candidate misses it by 7.1** (crown 9.4 against a limit of 16.5). A threshold that failed the thing it was written for and nothing else would be fitted to a result; this one was tested against both. **(6) THE EFFECT, INCLUDING THE UNCOMFORTABLE HALF:** the `short` candidate is now **10/11 and REJECTED**. Its width, matte, connectivity, legibility and fit on the skull are all still correct — the single failure is the crown height, and the gate's detail says so (`xOk: true, topOk: false`). **The approved afro is untouched at 9/9**, its file and SHA unchanged, and it passed under the old rule too — so this is not a rule fitted to reject one candidate. Six production rounds have now been spent on `short` without a usable asset; that is the honest position and it is not improved by a gate that cannot see the defect. **(7) THE STYLE SET STAYS AT SEVEN (owner, 2026-08-30).** The concept sheet reviewed this session shows seven styles, but **not the same seven**: it adds `Curtain`, `Spiky` and `Side Sweep`, and omits `long`, `buzz` and `afro`. **The owner declined to extend the set.** D-104's seven stand unchanged. The three extra styles are **not** adopted, have no C2 silhouette and no measured envelope, and are recorded here only so the decision is not silently revisited. **(8) THE CONCEPT SHEET IS ART DIRECTION, NOT ASSETS — MEASURED, NOT ASSUMED:** one flat 1536×1024 PNG, roughly 150×270 px per figure, hair fused to faces and t-shirts, with an alpha channel carrying **255 distinct values and not one pixel at exactly 255** and corners at alpha 0 holding brown RGB. A real asset is 1024×1536 for ONE hairstyle alone on transparency. Its embedded "Kvalitet & stil" checklist — *"ingen løse pixels eller glorie"*, *"korrekt gennemsigtighed"*, *"klar til brug i R2"* — is text an image model drew, not a measurement, and must not be read as one. It remains **useful**: it is the clearest statement of the character the owner wants, and it independently corroborates §(2). **(9) WHAT IS STILL MISSING:** **one of seven styles exists as an R2 raster** (`afro`). `short`, `tousled`, `curly`, `long`, `ponytail` and `buzz` have none. The round-1 candidates that would have covered them were all 1.6–2.1× oversized (D-111 §3, a prompt defect) and their files were lost with a deleted worktree; a disk-wide and git-history search this session found no surviving copy. The prompt defect **is** fixed — the afro proves it — so the gap is six generation requests that have never been made, not a technical blocker. **(10) EVIDENCE:** 665 unit tests — 660 pass, 4 skipped, **1 failing: the pre-existing `@electric-sql/pglite` module gap, unrelated and present on `main`**. 37 gate tests (13 new, covering the bound in both directions, the per-style behaviour, the crown/height band, and a counterfactual showing the pre-D-116 x-only rule accepts the fixture the two-axis rule refuses), 17 fit-measure tests (4 new, including the four-number re-derivation for all seven styles), 16 runtime-asset tests. `build:cloudflare` green. No golden baseline moved. **(11) BOUNDARIES:** two tools, three test files and documentation. **Zero image requests. No promotion, no manifest, resolver, z or transform change, no `AVATAR_R2` change, no asset added to or removed from `assets/avatar-r2/`, no prompt change, no geometric edit, no candidate deleted or moved, no existing threshold altered, no style added or removed, no Supabase, Storage, migration, RLS or Edge Function touched, no student record read or written.** Every student's avatar renders exactly as it did before. (branch `feat/r2-hair-top-bound-gate`) (2026-08-30) |
| **D-117** | **OWNER DECISION — the generator's `top` anchor is DERIVED from the gate's measured crown, not hand-measured alongside it (2026-08-30).** Status **`GENERATOR_GEOMETRY_DERIVED — NO_REQUEST_SENT`**. A correction to the D-116 contract, found while preparing to spend money against it; D-116's body is not rewritten. **(1) THE DEFECT:** the generator derives `w` and `low` from `STYLE_TARGETS` precisely so prompt and gate cannot drift apart — but `top` was a **separate hand-measurement of the same property**, taken in round 3. Once D-116 gave the gate its own measured `highestY`, the prompt was asking for one number while the gate enforced another. **(2) WHAT THAT COST, MEASURED BEFORE PAYING:** the margin between what the prompt requests and where the gate refuses ranged from 4.92 C2 units (`tousled`) down to **0.96** (`curly`) — and `afro` 0.38. The model had overshot `short`'s anchor by **ten** units the round before; asking it to hit `curly` inside one unit of a 240-unit canvas was buying a predictable failure. **(3) THE FIX:** `top = highestY / 240 × 100`, so every style now has exactly `Y_TOLERANCE` (4 units) of headroom instead of a per-style accident. New anchors: `short` 7.9→8.5, `tousled` 5.8→5.4, `curly` 4.6→5.9, `long` 7.1→7.7, `ponytail` 7.5→8.1, `buzz` 8.3→9.0. **The template is untouched** — byte for byte the round-4 file the accepted afro came from; only the interpolated geometry moved. **`afro` keeps its fix-round values** (w 40 / top 1 / low 18), which produced the shipped asset; re-deriving them would undo an owner decision. **(4) GUARDED:** the derivation test now covers all three numbers, a new test asserts the headroom equals `Y_TOLERANCE` for all six as BEHAVIOUR rather than arithmetic in a comment, another asserts no anchor sits below the bald skull it must cover, and a third proves the six render distinct complete prompts — a silent duplicate would be a paid duplicate. A stale sentinel that hard-coded `7.9` fired correctly and was made derivation-based rather than re-typed. **(5) EVIDENCE:** 668 unit tests — 663 pass, 4 skipped, 1 failing (the pre-existing `@electric-sql/pglite` gap, unrelated). 18 generator tests. All six styles dry-run clean with no network. **(6) BOUNDARIES:** one geometry table, one test file, this entry. **ZERO image requests were sent — the six remain un-generated and this change only prepares them.** No promotion, no manifest, resolver or asset change, no template edit, no threshold or envelope change, no Supabase or production effect. (branch `fix/r2-hair-generator-top-anchor`) (2026-08-30) |
| **D-118** | **OWNER DECISION — `clears-the-eye-line` must mean the EYES ARE CLEAR, not merely that the hair begins above them (2026-08-31).** Status **`EYE_COVERAGE_GATED — ZERO_IRIS_PIXELS`**. Successor to D-115/D-116; no earlier body is rewritten — only D-116's status line is re-pointed here. **(1) THE BLIND SPOT.** The gate read only `topByCol` over the forehead band: *where the hair first appears*. It never asked where the hair **ended**. A mass beginning at y 33 and continuing straight down over the face therefore passed. **(2) HOW IT SURFACED.** The image-guided `short` canary (2026-08-31) covered **100 % of the iris and 79 % of the face** — the child's face was invisible when mounted — and `clears-the-eye-line` reported ✓. Re-measured across every hair layer that exists, **`startsAboveEyeLine` is `true` for all of them**: the old condition never discriminated. Four of six candidates were covering the iris undetected — `tousled` 17 px, `long` 238, `ponytail` 634, the canary 1156 of 1156. **(3) THE FIX, AND ITS HARDENING:** the gate now also requires that no hair pixel over the iris has **any alpha at all**. The first implementation counted pixels at `alpha >= ALPHA_INK` and required 0 — which did not mean "no hair over the iris" but "no OPAQUE hair over the iris". **A continuous veil at alpha 127 could black out the whole pupil and pass**, because not one of its pixels reached the ink threshold. The limit is therefore on **max alpha over the iris**, not on a count above a threshold. `MAX_IRIS_PX_COVERED` is retired in favour of `MAX_IRIS_ALPHA = 0`, and the detail additionally reports pixels touched, pixels opaque and the **alpha-weighted obscured fraction** — the quantity the eye responds to, which makes "99.2 % obscured" and "0.08 % obscured" legible as different problems. Both conditions must hold; the detail reports which failed. **Measured at every level on the real decoded runtime assets, four layers are IRIS-CLEAN** — northstar, afro, buzz and short are at max alpha 0 and 0.00 % obscured, against tousled 248 / 1.62 %, long 254 / 20.38 %, ponytail 254 / 54.43 % and the guided canary 254 / 99.20 %. **IRIS-CLEAN IS NOT THE SAME AS VISUALLY APPROVED**, and the four are not one category: `northstar` is an approved positive control; `afro` is the OWNER-APPROVED positive control; `buzz` is iris-clean but **visually REJECTED for the needle tips at the ears**; `short` is iris-clean but rejected by other geometric gates (the D-116 crown bound). Only the first two are approved artwork — the other two merely leave the pupil alone. `tousled`, `long`, `ponytail` and the guided canary cover the iris and are rejected by this gate. **(4) THE EYES ARE THE ARTWORK'S OWN, NOT A NUMBER:** `tools/avatar/fixtures/r2-hair/eye-iris-mask-v1.png` (`8e7f2ff3…`, 1 156 ink px) is the shipped `eyes-neutral-iris-v1.webp` (`0187788c…`) thresholded at `ALPHA_INK` and tracked as a fixture, so the gate stays pure JS and CI-safe — decoding the .webp would need the vendored binary and drag the whole gate suite out of CI. The mask is SHA-verified on load; a swap is refused. Coverage is sampled proportionally, so the 512×768 runtime asset and a small test canvas ask the same question of the same anatomy. **(5) WHY THE IRIS AND NOT THE WHOLE EYE, AND WHY ZERO:** the approved afro grazes **4.7 %** of the full eye layer (lid/lash corner) and **0.0 %** of the iris — gating the full eye at zero would reject an asset the owner has already accepted. The iris is the part you see out of, hair renders at **z40 in front of the eyes** (`R2_STACK_Z`), and there is no defensible amount of hair over a pupil. **The case for a fail-closed zero** is that the two APPROVED positive controls sit at exactly zero alpha over the iris; the other two iris-clean layers show zero is technically achievable rather than aspirational; hair over the pupil is a visible conflict; and a future faint overlap pixel should be **reported for the owner to judge** rather than automatically loosening the threshold. **Deliberately NOT claimed:** that any surviving pixel is intentional artwork — connected antialiasing can survive the served cleanup without an artist meaning to place it. That is why the detail carries the exact max alpha and obscured fraction. **(6) EFFECT:** afro **9/9** unchanged, `buzz` 11/11 unchanged (**still visually rejected for the ear tips — this changes nothing there**), `short` 10/11 unchanged; `tousled` 9→8, `long` 8→7, `ponytail` 8→7, canary 9→8. **(7) A TEST FIXTURE WAS CORRECTED, NOT WEAKENED:** `goodShort`'s bottom edge moved 54→50. At 54 the synthetic rectangle reached 1.5 units into the iris and covered 9 of its pixels; the old gate could not see that, so it was called clean. A solid slab across the forehead down to the eyes was never clean hair — C2 `short`'s central hairline sits near y 33 and only its temples reach 56. **(8) EVIDENCE:** 674 unit tests — 669 pass, 4 skipped, 1 failing (the pre-existing `@electric-sql/pglite` gap, unrelated). 43 gate tests (6 new, including a counterfactual proving the pre-D-118 rule accepts the very fixture the new rule refuses), 16 runtime-asset tests. **(9) A SELF-INFLICTED FAULT, RECORDED:** two source files were corrupted mid-change by a PowerShell 5.1 `Get-Content`/`Set-Content` round-trip, which reads as ANSI and silently mojibakes every non-ASCII character (54 and 39 lines, plus BOMs). It surfaced as a spawn-based CLI test failing to match `/✖ within-style-envelope/`. Repaired by inverting the cp1252 decode; the inversion was verified exact and `git diff` confirmed only intended lines remained. **(10) THE SOURCE→FIXTURE BINDING IS EXECUTABLE, NOT DOCUMENTARY:** a CI-safe test hashes the shipped `eyes-neutral-iris-v1.webp` against `IRIS_SOURCE_SHA256`, and companion tests pin the mask's own SHA, its 512×768 dimensions and its 1 156 ink pixels. **WHAT THAT PROVES, STATED PRECISELY:** the pair detects drift in EITHER file — source or fixture — so `IRIS_SOURCE_SHA256` is enforced rather than decorative. **It does NOT, on its own, prove mathematically that the mask was DERIVED from that source**, because CI never decodes the .webp — that needs the vendored binary. The derivation proof is the reproducible local regeneration: `node tools/avatar/build/_view/make-iris-mask.mjs`, after which both SHAs are updated together, deliberately. **(11) BOUNDARIES:** one gate, one tracked fixture, one test file, this entry. **Zero image requests. No promotion, no manifest, resolver, z or transform change, no `AVATAR_R2` change, no asset added to or removed from `assets/avatar-r2/`, no prompt change, no threshold loosened, no Supabase or production effect.** (branch `feat/r2-hair-eyes-not-covered`) (2026-08-31) |
| **D-119** | **OWNER APPROVAL — the `short` R2 hair asset is promoted; second per-style asset (2026-08-31).** Status **`PROMOTED — LIVE_ON_DEPLOY`**. Successor to D-114 (afro, the first); the staged-fallback decision recorded there is unchanged and is not re-litigated here. **(1) WHAT WAS APPROVED.** The owner signed off VISUALLY at real render scale, on the avatar, quoting the artefact by hash: `assets/avatar-r2/hair/hair-short-v1.webp`, 512×768, 14,874 B, sha256 `1d01bfef787fc2a185e03f40abe5695a186edee41bcca2268afaa7209d41e9f3`. The approval names those exact pixels, so those exact pixels are what shipped — the promoted file is byte-identical to the reviewed one, re-verified before commit. **(2) WHY THIS CANDIDATE EXISTS AT ALL.** Rounds 1–5 and the first image-guided canary all drew the hair ~1.8× too wide on the full 1024×1536 authoring flade. The tight-crop canary tested ONE hypothesis: that the model mis-scales when the head occupies a small share of the frame. It generated inside a square 512×512 crop around the head (authoring x 256–768, y 10–522 = C2 x 40–120, y 1.56–81.56; bottom pinned to the neck line so torso cannot enter), then mapped back with the PINNED `downscaleHalf` because 512→1024 is an exact ×2. Result: envelope width **54.53 C2 vs the 56 target = 0.974×**. The hypothesis held. Honest limit: the prompt had to be rewritten for the new framing, so this is `crop + adapted prompt`, not a clean single-variable A/B. **One paid image request; no retry, no fallback model.** **(3) GATES.** 11/11 on the unchanged pipeline (authoring cleanup → `downscaleHalf` → served cleanup → cwebp -lossless -exact -z 9 → dwebp → byte-identity). Iris max alpha **0** (D-118), one component, zero specks, orphan-soft 11 against the served budget of 16, decoded = reference byte for byte. **No gate, threshold, envelope or pipeline step was changed or loosened.** **(4) PRODUCTION EFFECT — REAL, AND CORRECTED FROM A FALSE CLAIM.** The first version of this entry, the code comment beside the manifest key and the PR description all stated the promotion was *dormant because `AVATAR_R2` is false*. **That was wrong**, and it was wrong in the direction that matters: it understated the effect of merging. `js/avatar-layers.js` carries **`export const AVATAR_R2 = true`** — default ON since **D-101 (2026-08-08)** — and the LIVE served file at `https://lærlig.dk/js/avatar-layers.js` was fetched with a cache-buster and is **byte-identical to `origin/main`**, carrying `AVATAR_R2 = true` at line 332. The error came from trusting a stale note instead of reading the constant; the served-code check is now part of the promotion routine. **THE ACTUAL BEHAVIOUR, PROBED READ-ONLY UNDER PRODUCTION DEFAULTS (no test override, no localStorage opt-out):** `isAvatarR2()` returns `true`; `r2StackSrcsFor` resolves for neutral × medium — which is also what an identity with absent/invalid fields defaults to — so a normal user is on **R2**. `isAvatarR2()` is false ONLY for the exact string `localStorage.avatar_r2 === "0"`; `"1"`, `""`, `"true"` and a missing key all fall through to ON. C2 fallback happens for an unsupported identity (explicit male/female body type, or any tone but medium → `r2StackSrcsFor` returns `null`) or a failed mandatory layer (D-062 atomic gate). **So on merge + deploy, a student with raw stored `hairstyle="short"` on neutral × medium gets `/assets/avatar-r2/hair/hair-short-v1.webp` on their next load — immediately, with no flag flip and no data migration.** Verified in the same probe: that identity's `stack.hair` is the promoted asset, is not `northstar`, is not `null`, and does not drop to C2. **What does NOT change:** the five styles without artwork (`tousled`, `curly`, `long`, `ponytail`, `buzz`) still resolve to `northstar`; the hairstyle picker stays hidden on R2, because `avatar.html`'s `hairstyleShapesSupported()` returns `activeRenderPath !== "r2"` and never reads the manifest, so producing artwork cannot re-enable it (D-102 holds); no stored identity, profile row or student record is written; no resolver logic changed — `hairSrcForR2` has been style-aware since D-114. **Pre-merge production state confirmed by read-only HTTP:** `hair-northstar-v1.webp` 200, `hair-afro-v1.webp` 200, **`hair-short-v1.webp` 404** — Short is not in production until this PR merges. **(5) SURFACED, NOT FIXED — the alias gap.** On C2 the legacy values `default` and `braid` ALIAS to `short` and `ponytail`; the R2 path has no alias table and matches the RAW stored value, so a student stored as `default` sees `northstar` on R2 and short hair on C2. Pre-existing, widened in visibility by this promotion, deliberately NOT changed here: closing it is a decision about stored data, not a side effect of shipping artwork. Pinned by a test comment. **(6) PROVENANCE IS TRACKED.** The 1024×1536 authoring source is committed as `tools/avatar/fixtures/r2-hair/short-crop-authoring.png` (sha `ce789b49…`), and a local binary-dependent test REBUILDS the asset from it and requires the shipped bytes — so the approved artwork is reproducible from a fresh clone rather than from a surviving scratch directory (the D-111 §9 failure). **(7) SENTINEL FIRED AS DESIGNED.** `Short is NOT promoted — no hair asset exists for it` failed on this change, which is what it was for: the asset arrived through an approval, not around one. It was rewritten to guard the reproducibility claim that replaces it, not deleted. **(8) THE REJECTED WORK STAYS REJECTED.** Both salvage finalists and every earlier Short attempt remain negative examples and must not be used or promoted. **(9) NEXT.** `tousled` must be visibly more uneven and voluminous than this, or the two styles collapse into one. **(10) BOUNDARIES:** one asset, one tracked source, one manifest key, three test files, this entry. **No resolver change, no z or transform change, no `AVATAR_R2` change, no gate or threshold change, no prompt change, no golden rebaselined, no hairstyle-picker/UI change, no Supabase, database or student-data mutation, zero image requests.** The one thing this PR DOES change in production on deploy is stated in §4 and is the point of it: a student storing `short` starts seeing the approved Short asset. (branch `feat/r2-hair-short-promotion`) (2026-08-31) |
| **D-120** | **OWNER DECISION — ONE review-only image request may explore a NEW North Star on the approved D geometry, as a narrow exception to D-033 / D-034 / D-040 / D-042 (2026-09-04).** Status **`EXCEPTION_GRANTED — REVIEW_ONLY — NO_PROMOTION`**. Successor to **D-042** on one question only — *whether a new avatar may be generated at all*. D-042 is **not rewritten**, per the register convention, and every one of its other constraints stands. **(1) THE CONFLICT THIS RESOLVES, STATED PLAINLY.** D-042 forbids "full AI regeneration, redesign, **'make a new avatar,'** and any unmasked/broad prompt", and forbids any change to head size, eye shape, hair silhouette, pose, outfit style, skin tone or **proportions**. The authorised exploration is exactly that: a freely generated new figure at **ca. 4.6 heads** instead of the Master's ca. 4.09. **Six of D-042's seven prohibitions are hit simultaneously** — this is not a grey zone and was not treated as one. It was reported as a blocking conflict, the register was left untouched, and **no image request has been sent**. Analysis: `tools/avatar/build/northstar-geometry-v02/d-control-set/d-decision-conflict.md`. **(2) DECISION A — THE GENERATOR IS NAMED AND PINNED.** **OpenAI Image API**, endpoint **`https://api.openai.com/v1/images/edits`**, model **`gpt-image-2`**, with **exactly four input images in this order**: **Image 1** `geometry-reference/geometry-reference-transparent.png` (40,247 B, sha256 `e3c44a512bec3f3780e5b9d99a61cba473364e6889c1a5a6a7b0b99bca552dc7`) · **Image 2** `geometry-reference/geometry-silhouette.png` (26,362 B, sha256 `0e760fb1b0ced293654ece10027c1fbdf68ee15aee4beaedf6fcbc81b7bf602a`) · **Image 3** `geometry-reference/geometry-plate-bald-nude.png` (26,826 B, sha256 `d3a855fc50afe634f135bf306a68404a7a6b4bd4ac52d8a931cf65510b413e11`) · **Image 4** `style-identity-reference/style-identity-reference-head.png` (367,948 B, sha256 `abf551ae169d6b2c8e1253d971c6bb9b9571260fcfd95d7676bb600ff33b6640`), all under `tools/avatar/build/northstar-geometry-v02/d-control-set/`. Parameters: **`n=1`, `quality=high`, `size=1024x1536`, `output_format=png`, `background=transparent`, NO mask**. **`input_fidelity` MUST NOT be sent** — `gpt-image-2` handles image input at high fidelity automatically and rejects the parameter; this is not an assumption but a recorded repo finding (`invalid_input_fidelity_model`, tried and removed 2026-07-24, noted in `tools/avatar/build-face-expr-d042.mjs`). **No retry, no fallback model, no automatic prompt modification.** The prompt is the byte-locked master prompt `d-master-prompt.md` (12,330 B, sha256 `6b0ab86e65c934cbbb8396379fabc098b9d914d5da43bd4ae35342935f12007f`), which addresses the attachments as Image 1–4 and states that Images 1–3 win over Image 4 on every proportion. **(3) DECISION B — B1: THE SHIPPED NORTH STAR STAYS AUTHORITATIVE.** **D-032 stands unchanged.** `assets/avatar/reference/Northstar Master.png` remains the sole authoritative geometric source for everything in production. The D construction is a **parallel, non-authoritative experimental track**: nothing produced under this exception may be cited as geometry for any production decision, and if the track is abandoned nothing has to be undone. This entry overrides the prior decisions **only** as far as is strictly necessary for: one review-only image request · one returned candidate · local measurement and visual review. It authorises **no** automatic acceptance, **no** runtime registration, **no** manifest change, **no** resolver change, **no** replacement of the existing North Star, and **no** promotion without a later separate owner decision and PR. **(4) THE REQUEST CONTRACT — BEFORE THE CALL.** A later execution MUST, before spending anything: recompute SHA-256 for all four input images and for the exact master prompt and compare them to §2; serialise the request configuration deterministically to disk; confirm `OPENAI_API_KEY` is present **without printing its value**; and **STOP before payment if a single byte, parameter or precondition differs**. **(5) THE REQUEST CONTRACT — AFTER THE CALL.** Fetch and persist the raw result immediately, before any other step; record request id, model, every parameter, timestamp, byte count and SHA-256; preserve the complete non-secret API response metadata; verify dimensions and transparency; run the existing geometric and visual measurements; mount the candidate at **180×270, 112×168, 72×108 and 52×78**; build a fully self-contained offline review page; compare against the D geometry and against the existing North Star; then **stop — no cleanup, no retouching, no promotion, no commit, no production effect.** **(6) WHAT MAY NEVER BE SENT.** Only the four images in §2, the byte-locked prompt and the fixed parameters. **NOT** the old full-body North Star (`style-identity-reference/style-identity-reference.png` — REVIEW ONLY; it shows the yellow star, green top, orange sleeve stripes, green wristbands, cargo pocket, green sneakers and the OLD body geometry, all of which a generator can copy regardless of prompt wording), **NOT** technical overlays, masks, measurement tables, review screenshots, crop documentation, silhouette comparisons beyond Image 2, and **NOT** any other candidate or runtime asset. **(7) THE GEOMETRY IS FIXED BEFORE THE REQUEST, NOT AFTER.** Construction **D**, all values in the C2 160×240 system: axis x = **80** · crown y = **31.56** · bottom y = **235.94** · figure height **204.38** · head height **44.43** · **ca. 4.6 heads** · cranium width **41.94** · neck y = **75.99** · shoulder width **54** · shoulder/cranium **1.29** · iris y = **57.48** · iris diameter **7.08** · eye-centre distance **19.3** · waistband **121.78** · t-shirt hem **130.58** · hip **140.12** · crotch **151.12** · knee **183.53** · ankle **215.94** · shoe shaft **220.94** · sole **232.34** · shoe height **20** · arm thickness **9.25** · arm–torso air **3.75** · hand width **10.25** · legs **41.5 %** of the figure. Binding machine-readable source: `d-control-set-spec.json` (49,786 B, sha256 `348c4faa9e7a668d14f5dbb230fad95eb5c466ce82beda2503e6f07808284a19`), which carries all 26 measures verified 26/26 and the whole geometry object deep-compared identical across rebuilds. **Image 4's provenance:** a pure crop from the official Master (sha256 `2ca10ef868b9564164f28afc8bb03baec99cc10fd03f7200ed2dc58edd607a21`) at **x 240, y 20, 545×505**, with **275,225 of 275,225 pixels byte-identical to the source**, **zero** garment-green pixels, no padding, no resampling, no retouching — full hair, face, both ears, eyes, brows, nose, mouth and neck; the bottom edge sits one row above the first green garment pixel (y 525). **(8) THE TARGET IMAGE.** A boy of about 8–10 years · warm tan skin · large brown eyes · dark brown tousled hair · a friendly, clearly childlike expression · fully front-facing, symmetrical, neutral standing pose · a plain mid-grey short-sleeve t-shirt · muted mid-blue jeans · light or mid-grey sneakers · **no logos, patterns, jewellery, wristbands, cargo pockets or green clothing of any kind.** **(9) THE INTEGRATION GAP, SURFACED AND NOT FIXED HERE.** `gpt-image-2` at `1024x1536` is already proven reachable in this repo — `tools/avatar/build-face-expr-d042.mjs` calls `images/edits` on the pinned snapshot `gpt-image-2-2026-04-21` (with a mask), and `tools/avatar/openai-generate-hair-item.mjs` pins the same snapshot on `images/generations`. But **no adapter today sends four input images to `images/edits` with no mask**, and `openai-generate-item.mjs` and `openai-generate-torso-item.mjs` still default to `gpt-image-1` via `OPENAI_IMAGE_MODEL`; the OpenAI SDK is not installed, so every adapter uses raw `fetch`. Writing that adapter is a **separate** piece of work and **no adapter is changed by this entry or its PR**. If the installed integration cannot call `gpt-image-2`, execution **MUST STOP and report the blockage** — it may **never** silently fall back to `gpt-image-1` or any other model. **(10) PROVENANCE IS REQUIRED BECAUSE THE EVIDENCE AREA IS GITIGNORED.** The control set and any candidate live under `tools/avatar/build/`, which is gitignored. **D-113 recorded that exactly such an approved artefact was destroyed by an authorised worktree cleanup and recovered only by luck.** Therefore the raw returned bytes and their SHA-256 are recorded at the moment of receipt, before any cleanup step, and any later reference to "the candidate" must quote that hash. **(11) BOUNDARIES — what this entry does NOT touch:** no change to `assets/avatar-r2`, `R2_MANIFEST`, any resolver, `AVATAR_R2`, any gate, threshold, envelope or golden; no promotion, no tracked source, no manifest key; no adapter or SDK change; no change to the seven-hairstyle set (D-116) or the eye-coverage rule (D-118); no Supabase, database or student-data mutation; no production write; no change to the official Master, which stays byte-identical. D-033, D-034, D-040 and D-042 remain in force for **everything except** this single named exploration, and D-032 is untouched per §3. **(12) WHAT THIS ENTRY DOES NOT DECIDE:** whether the D geometry is adopted; whether the resulting figure replaces the current North Star; whether the Master is ever re-cut; how a future decomposition into rig layers would work; and whether a second request may ever be made. Each is a separate decision requiring its own entry. **HONEST LIMIT:** D-033 was written after four regenerations and one inpaint attempt all drifted taller and leaner with altered hair and face, and concluded that proportions cannot be locked by regeneration. **That finding is not disputed and is not weakened here.** It is *expected* that the generator will not hit 4.6 heads exactly; nothing in this entry promises otherwise. The difference is that drift was then a failure, because identity had to be preserved, and here it is the measured variable, because identity is meant to change — so the mitigation is measurement against the control set, not optimism. (branch `docs/d-120-northstar-d-generation`) (2026-09-04) |

`main` and then deleted** (local + `origin`) during the 2026-07-31 repository cleanup, so the names no

### Deleted branches — tip-commit reference (2026-08-28)

A second cleanup, on the same terms as the 2026-07-31 one above. These 23 branches were **merged
into `main` and then deleted** (local + `origin`), so their names no longer resolve as refs.
**No content was lost** — each tip below was verified an ancestor of `origin/main` immediately
before deletion **and again afterwards** (`git merge-base --is-ancestor <sha> origin/main`).
Look the work up with `git show <sha>` / `git log <sha>`, or recreate the pointer with
`git branch <name> <sha>`.

Four tips are merge commits from syncing the branch with `main`, so their own subject says
little; the sha still reaches the whole branch. Two branches were deliberately **kept**:
`feat/teacher-mediated-password-help` (not merged — 2 commits on `origin`, and a third,
`8fe26b2`, that exists only in a local worktree and has never been pushed) and
`feat/avatar-r2-default-activation-d101` (merged, but still has a live worktree).

## Current Production State (avatar MVP path — D-040)
- **Tier-0 base = `Northstar Master.png`** (fixed default avatar; no reconstruction needed for MVP).
- **MVP shop = accessory overlays only** (aura, back, headwear, face/masks, eyes/glasses).
- **Blocking next step = 164L** (the deterministic non-AI extraction tooling; 164K spec locked by D-041).
- **Deferred upgrades (not MVP blockers):** neutral reconstructed base (164B/164I/D-039),
  per-user skin tone / hairstyle / hair-color, torso/bottom/shoes clothing slots.

### 167A raster migration — execution path LOCKED (2026-07-01)
- **Step 1 scaffold shipped** (commit `8e089af`): `assets/avatar-r2/` + empty `R2_MANIFEST` + inert
  raster resolvers in `js/avatar-layers.js` (`AVATAR_R2=false`; render on C2/SVG). Additive; no
  render/engine/z/identity change.
- **Path LOCKED = D-040 Phase-1 "Master-as-is" FIRST**, then 163F Phase-2 decomposition later
  (**Phase-2 not started**). Step-3 wiring plan: [`167a-step3-render-wiring-plan.md`](./167a-step3-render-wiring-plan.md).
- **Step 3a DONE (2026-07-01):** the Phase-1 baked base is produced + wired. A deterministic extractor
  (`tools/avatar/extract-master-base.mjs`) alpha-cuts + downscales the Master → a **temporary transparent
  PNG preview** `assets/avatar-r2/base/body-neutral-medium-v1.png`, registered in `R2_MANIFEST`
  (`base:{ "neutral-medium":{ v:1, ext:"png" } }`, `version:1`) and consumed by `mountC2Avatar` **only
  when `AVATAR_R2` is true (default false → untouched C2/SVG render)**. Engines/cosmetics/identity
  unchanged; C2 fallback intact. Validated (node --check, unit, smoke).
- **PNG is temporary; WebP is the production target (D-013)** — no image dependency was added (no
  sharp/cwebp). **WebP swap DEFERRED (2026-07-01):** no encoder is available in the environment
  (cwebp/ffmpeg/ImageMagick/sharp all absent) and adding a dep / hand-rolling an encoder was declined
  to avoid new deps. The **PNG (~244 KB, within the <350 KB budget) is the current Phase-1 runtime
  asset**; WebP swap (`{ v:2, ext:"webp" }`) waits until a proper encoder is available.
  > **UPDATE 2026-07-02:** an encoder is now available (vendored libwebp `cwebp.exe`, gate 4 — see
  > the Phase-2 gate list below). The Phase-1 WebP swap is therefore **unblocked**, but remains a
  > separate, deliberate action (encode `body-neutral-medium-v2.webp`, register `{ v:2, ext:"webp" }`);
  > not done here.
- **Phase-1 engine guard DONE (2026-07-01):** when the raster base is active
  (`isAvatarR2ActiveFor(identity)`), expression + blink overlays are **skipped** at the mount sites
  (app.js, avatar.html, hub.html) so they never render over the baked face; **presence/breathing stays
  on**. Engine logic unchanged (caller-side skip only, an already-supported "engine absent" state);
  C2/SVG path untouched. Verified by DOM probe (raster → 0 expr overlays, no blink layer, breathing
  active) + smoke.
- **Phase-1 cosmetic slot-gate DONE (2026-07-01):** on the raster base, `composeR2Layers` renders only
  `R2_PHASE1_SAFE_SLOTS = ["aura","back"]` (behind-figure, anchor-independent). Head/face/eye
  (headwear/face/eyes) + clothing (torso/body/neck) are gated — QA showed they float on the legacy
  anchors / clash with the baked outfit. Raster path only; C2/SVG renders all slots unchanged. No
  z-model/anchor/mask/shop/ownership/identity change. Cosmetic re-anchoring = Phase-2.
- **Phase-1 visual sign-off = PASS (2026-07-01)** — see
  [`167a-phase1-visual-signoff-checklist.md`](./167a-phase1-visual-signoff-checklist.md).
- **Phase-1 pilot opt-in LIVE (2026-07-01, commit `78a8e6d`).** `isAvatarR2()` now honours a
  per-browser override `localStorage.avatar_r2 === "1"` (mirrors `AVATAR_V2`). **`AVATAR_R2` remains
  `false` by default** → non-pilot users still render the untouched **C2/SVG** avatar; only browsers
  that set the key see the raster. **No DB cohorting, no percentage rollout, no global enable.**
  - **Pilot selection:** neutral-medium identity + low/no cosmetics (only aura/back render on the
    baked base; head/face/eye/clothing are slot-gated). Criteria + onboarding:
    [`167a-phase1-pilot-rollout.md`](./167a-phase1-pilot-rollout.md).
  - **Opt-out:** `localStorage.removeItem("avatar_r2")` (reload) → back to C2 instantly.
  - **Still deferred:** WebP swap (no encoder; PNG is the current runtime asset). **Phase-2 163F
    decomposition NOT started.**
- **Remaining (deferred): Phase-2 163F decomposition** (steps 3b/3c — living face/eyes/blink, hair

  **Gate 2 is REOPENED (iter7 invalidated) — NOT satisfied.** Phase-2 runtime code may not start. The
  immediate blocker is the **base recovery** (D-043): re-register + neutralize + re-review the adopted
  `recovery-base-v1-blankface.png`; then the remaining art layers (gate 3) and the composed visual
  sign-off (gate 5). Throughout: **Phase-2 implementation is still NOT started**; no Phase-2 runtime
  code; **`AVATAR_R2` stays `false`**; the Phase-1 PNG baked base + pilot opt-in + C2/SVG fallback remain
  intact.
- **Art-production handoff written (2026-07-02, art policy revised D-042) — for gates 2–3.**
  [`167a-phase2-artist-handoff.md`](./167a-phase2-artist-handoff.md) is the practical art-production brief for
  the decomposed layers (v2 base + face/eyes/eyelid/hair): filenames/dims/transparent-bg, what stays
  identical to Master vs what is removed from the v2 base, the approved eye-box (§8), the 164B.3
  acceptance gate, and producer/delivery/rejection checklists. **Policy (D-042): AI-assisted masked
  decomposition allowed; AI regeneration/redesign forbidden.** Doc only — offline art deliverable; no
  runtime/manifest/flag change; Phase-2 not started.

## Current Risks
- R-1 (Medium): Art-direction drift — flat SVG base ≠ North Star (eyes/finish). Being
  resolved by the Hybrid Raster re-asset.
- R-2 (Medium): Onboarding mismatch — users cannot pick C2 vocabulary.
- R-3 (Low): Visual/cosmetics regression — mitigated by goldens + flag OFF.
- R-4 (Low-op): Migration-history drift (ledger) — `db push` blocked; MCP apply only.
- R-5 (Low-op): Two-clone sync discipline (ff-pull after each push).
- R-6 (High, art): AI style drift + decomposition seams across raster assets — mitigate
  by producing all layers from one North Star + style-lock + gatekeeper + golden QA.
  **CONFIRMED for the base (164C):** **unmasked** AI generation/regeneration reliably drifts the
  base's proportions/identity. **Mitigation revised (D-042, 2026-07-02):** since no human painter is
  available, the base + rig are produced by **AI-assisted MASKED decomposition on the frozen Master**
  (masked inpainting/outpainting that lifts features + reconstructs what they hid, identity-locked to
  the Master) — **not** free regeneration, which stays forbidden. Residual drift risk is caught by the
  **164B.3 gate** + composed visual sign-off (gate 5). (Old rule: manual paint-over only, D-033.)
- R-7 (Medium, tech): Hair/iris tint quality (canvas multiply + fixed highlight) —
  mitigate via hybrid tint + hand-painted overrides; prototype early.
- R-8 (Medium, art/process): Style coherence across a 1000+ AI-generated item catalog
  (R-6 applied to Tier 2) — mitigate via the locked style kit + per-slot masks + the
  slot-mask/QA gates + style-conformance scoring (D-034 / `docs/164d-shop-pipeline.md`).
- R-9 (Medium, product): MVP identity deferral (D-040) — Tier-0 = Master as a **single
  fixed avatar** + accessories; per-user skin tone / hairstyle / hair-color variation is
  **deferred** with the neutral-base upgrade. Accepted for the pilot MVP; revisit if
  per-user identity at launch becomes a hard requirement.

- TD-3: Migration-history drift; repo migration files ≠ DB version ledger. **Accepted, not resolved** — see D-110 and [`migration-workflow-policy.md`](./migration-workflow-policy.md); `db push` is forbidden until a separate normalisation task.
- TD-4: Gitlink anomaly in ROOT clone (vestigial, not on deploy path).
- TD-5: Local prod-data snapshot `backups/…` kept out of repo (no `.gitignore` yet).
- TD-6: Flat-SVG C2 asset set is **superseded** by the Hybrid Raster pipeline (kept
